Groups now bucket under collapsible category headers derived from the group name's prefix — the token before the earliest `|` or `:` delimiter, trimmed (e.g. "CA", "UK", "US", "USA", "Sports"); a field-value survey of the live `/api/stream-groups` response showed both delimiter conventions in production use, so restricting to `|` alone (as originally scoped) would have dumped ~35 of 137 live groups into "Other" for no reason. Groups with no recognizable prefix fall into a final "Other" bucket. Categories default to collapsed and persist expand/collapse state per session in `localStorage` (`ecm-streams-category-expanded`), matching the pane's existing filter-persistence idiom; "Expand all"/"Collapse all" now also expand/collapse every category. While a search is active, categories auto-expand to surface matches immediately without mutating the persisted collapse state. Category headers are real `<button>` elements with `aria-expanded` and a visible name+count label (name + group count), so the icon-only-button a11y audit passes with no extra `aria-label`. Categorization applies to the already-filtered (provider/group-filtered, search-filtered) visible set — existing behavior (group expand, drag-to-channel, provider/group filters, "Hide mapped", Edit Mode selection) is unchanged. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-09x38.5)

- **Logo Manager: sortable Name/Used-By columns + server-side "unused only" filter, at 2,946-logo scale (bead 09x38.13, build 0119).** Logo Manager previously loaded every logo into the browser on tab-open and paginated/searched client-side, with no way to sort and no way to isolate unused logos — bulk-identifying cleanup candidates across ~2,946 rows was impractical. Dispatcharr's own Logo API has no ordering support and its `search` param was silently a no-op (only `name`/`used`/`ids` are read), so `GET /api/channels/logos` gained `sort_by` (`name` | `channel_count`), `sort_order` (`asc` | `desc`), and `unused_only` params: when any of sort/filter/search is requested, ECM fetches the complete Dispatcharr logo list in one call (via Dispatcharr's `no_pagination=true` escape hatch) and sorts/filters/paginates it server-side, so results are truthful across pages; requests using none of those params keep the original zero-overhead single-page passthrough (LogoModal picker, AutoSyncSettingsModal, GuideTab, and `getAllLogos()` are unaffected). LogoManagerTab now fetches one page at a time instead of the full catalog, with clickable Name/Used By column headers (M3UChangesTab's sortable-header idiom) and an "Unused only" toggle that composes with search and pagination. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-09x38.13)

- **User guide: "Set Up Your First Channels" end-to-end workflow tutorial (bead gsnw0.16, build 0113).** New `docs/user_guide/getting-started/your-first-channels.md` walks a new operator through one continuous path — add an M3U account, add an EPG source, choose which stream groups to sync, refresh, then create channels, channel groups, and stream assignments in Channel Manager — with 12 screenshots captured per the user-guide screenshot conventions. Getting Started section status advances from Stub to In progress. Docs only, no functional change. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-gsnw0.16)

### Fixed
- **Two Settings controls gain programmatic labels for assistive technology (beads enhancedchannelmanager-db8ae + enhancedchannelmanager-in1o0, build 0174).** (1) The Full Backup ZIP file chooser (Settings → Backup & Restore → Restore Full Backup) had no associated label or accessible name — AT users couldn't identify a high-consequence restore control or its expected file type (WCAG 1.3.1, 3.3.2, 4.1.2). It now carries a visible, programmatically-associated label ("Choose ECM full-backup ZIP (.zip)", `htmlFor`/`id`), so the control's purpose and accepted .zip format are announced and `getByLabel` locates it uniquely. (2) The Authentication minimum-password-length number input (Settings → Authentication → Local Authentication) had a bare `<label>` with no `htmlFor` and an input with no id/aria attributes. The visible "Minimum Password Length" label is now associated via `htmlFor`/`id`, and the 6-32 constraint guidance is exposed to AT via `aria-describedby`. Component tests pin both associations (getByLabelText uniqueness, accessible description, and the labeled input still driving the save payload); rendered-browser verification via Playwright `getByLabel` passed at 1920x1080 and 1280x720 for both controls. No behavior change — label association and one CSS rule only.
- **Settings → M3U Digest: a failed settings load no longer retries in a tight loop or storms error toasts — it lands in a recoverable inline error state (bead enhancedchannelmanager-fi3dq, build 0173).** When `GET /api/m3u/digest/settings` (or the M3U-accounts fetch in the same batch) failed, the page-activation effect re-armed itself every time the loading flag flipped back to false with settings still null, re-issuing the request batch in an unbounded loop, pushing one error toast per failed attempt, and leaving the page stuck at "Loading digest settings…" with no way to recover. Two-part fix: (1) the digest page now latches a `digestError` state on failure — the activation effect stops re-issuing requests while it is set, so one failed page activation costs exactly one request batch and zero toasts — and renders a stable inline `role="alert"` error panel (the Channel Pipeline tab's existing error-state idiom) with the failure message, actionable guidance, and a keyboard-focusable Retry button that clears the latch and issues exactly one new batch; (2) the toast provider (`NotificationContext`) now deduplicates equivalent notifications (same type + title + message) app-wide — repeat callers get the existing toast's id back instead of stacking a duplicate — so no misbehaving caller can flood the viewport again (the visible-toast cap of 5 with a non-interactive "+N more" overflow indicator was already in place and is now pinned by test). Component tests cover the one-batch-per-activation, no-toast, retry-recovers, and retry-fails-again paths plus provider dedup/cap semantics; rendered-browser verification (injected digest-endpoint failure) confirmed one request per activation, zero toasts, in-viewport keyboard-reachable Retry, and full recovery at both 1920x1080 and 1280x720. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-fi3dq)
- **Flaky test: PendingMergesPage cancel-restore race (bead enhancedchannelmanager-6wkgm, build 0171).** The 'restores the original paginated view when a 51-row all action is cancelled' test raced its assertions against the post-cancel refetch: after clicking Cancel it `waitFor`'d the snapshot row "Stream 51" to disappear, but dialog close + refetch + re-render took ~1976ms on a loaded CI runner vs testing-library's 1000ms default `waitFor` timeout (1 failure in ~100 executions on dev-push run 30178431720). Test-only fix, same family as the earlier 681a1 `vi.waitFor` hardening: the test now first waits for the deterministic intermediate state (dialog removal), then waits for the paginated view's restoration — with the checkbox-count assertion moved inside the same `waitFor` so it can't fire before the re-render — under an explicit 5000ms timeout. No component behavior change; file verified green across 10 consecutive runs.
- **M3U change digest was blind to pure renames — a group whose stream COUNT stays the same but whose stream NAMES change now records the swap (bead enhancedchannelmanager-wawwh, build 0167).** `M3UChangeDetector.detect_changes` only ever compared prev/curr stream-name sets when a group's stream count actually changed, so a provider that swaps one dateless slot name for another in place (the PPV-slot pattern: "Event A" out, "Event B" in, count unchanged) recorded nothing in `M3UChangeLog` — silently invisible to the M3U Changes tab and digest notifications, both of which read `M3UChangeLog`. (The jqwfq stale-dateless guard is a separate code path and was never affected by this gap: it reads `M3USnapshot.groups_data` directly via `previous_day_names()`, and `create_snapshot()` writes that column in full on every refresh regardless of what `detect_changes` records to the change log.) The per-group prev/curr name sets were already being built unconditionally every refresh for every common group (not just count-changed ones), so this was a comparison the code was throwing away, not a missing fetch: `detect_changes` now also diffs those sets when the count is stable, but ONLY when both snapshots' TRUE group totals are below the per-group name-capture cap (`tasks.m3u_refresh.MAX_STREAM_NAMES`, 500) — a review round (PR #740) found that "both sides captured some names" alone is not sufficient: Dispatcharr's stream listing carries no explicit ordering, so a group at or above the cap can have its captured name list drawn from a different page each refresh with byte-identical real membership, which would otherwise fabricate a phantom rename. A capped group therefore stays silent here — a known, accepted limitation, matching the codebase's existing philosophy for this exact cap (`services/event_sync_staleness.py`: "the cap can cause missed detections, never false staleness"), not a claim that capped groups are covered. A second review round found the count-stable diff itself used set semantics, which collapse duplicate names — `["Event A", "Event A"]` → `["Event A", "Event B"]` recorded an addition with no matching removal, hiding the replaced duplicate occurrence; the diff now uses `collections.Counter` multiset subtraction so duplicate-name replacements are recorded correctly. Recorded as a normal `streams_added` + `streams_removed` pair per swap, so existing M3U Changes tab filters/rendering need no changes. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-wawwh)

### Changed
- **UX polish batch — 9 minors from walkthrough passes 1-3 (bead 09x38.15, build 0127).** A holding bead of small, independent findings from the 3-pass UX walkthrough, promoted together as one mechanical batch: (1) EPG Manager rows sharing a priority now get an inline `.badge-warning` "tie" chip next to the priority number — previously the tie was only explained via the column-header tooltip. (3) Channel Manager's Channel List Filters panel gains a "Clean up empty groups…" link that navigates to Settings → Maintenance → Orphaned Channel Groups (a new `ecm:navigate-settings-maintenance` window event, mirroring the existing `ecm:open-task-editor` cross-tab navigation pattern) rather than embedding the scan/delete tool inline. (4) The two same-named "Debug Bundle" actions are disambiguated: Channel Pipeline's header button is now "Pipeline Debug Bundle" (with a tooltip pointing at the whole-app one), and General Settings' is now "Generate App Debug Bundle" with copy stating its whole-app scope. (5) Normalization groups list now reads "1 rule" (singular) instead of "1 rules". (6) The Standard Rule editor's Output & Run step gains an inline orange warning (matching the adjacent merge-scope control's `.norm-hint` idiom) when Orphan Cleanup is on its destructive default ("Delete orphaned channels"). (7) Backup & Restore gains a "Which one do I need?" card giving top-level guidance across the three overlapping-sounding restore mechanisms — YAML Export, DBAS Backup artifact, and legacy Full Backup — before the cards that implement each. (8) The Guide tab's EPG-grid channel-name column widens from 200px to 260px at 1280px+ viewports, giving names more room before truncating (native title tooltip remains the fallback below that width). (9) The Stats tab's ~10 stacked sections (4 conditional + 6 always-mounted panels) gain a "Jump to section" dropdown, reusing the `OverflowMenu` component (which gained an optional `icon` prop for this non-kebab use) with `scrollIntoView` targeting new per-section anchor ids; the dropdown only lists sections actually present in the DOM. (12) Backup export/restore section labels renamed for clarity: "Scheduled Tasks" → "Task Settings & Alerts" and "Task Schedules" → "Task Run Schedules" (`backend/routers/backup.py` `RESTORABLE_SECTIONS`, `frontend/src/components/BackupRestoreModal.tsx` `SECTION_LABELS`) — a prior engineering spike confirmed `ScheduledTask` and `TaskSchedule` are genuinely distinct entities, so this is a label-only rename, not a collapse. **Explicitly out of scope, reported not built:** item 2 (a contextual multi-select action bar on ChannelsPane) is a real design feature, promoted to its own bead rather than built as a minor; item 10 (M3U Changes row-drawer) had no trivial enrichment worth doing standalone; item 11 (Journal automated-entry volume) is a data-retention policy question for the PO. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-09x38.15)
- **Channel Pipeline: the Standard Rule editor becomes a true step-at-a-time wizard, matching the Event Sync editor's interaction model (bead 09x38.10, build 0125).** UX pass-2 (DOM-confirmed) found the Standard Rule editor and the Event Sync Rule editor shared the identical `.modal-stepper` pill strip but behaved oppositely: Event Sync is a true wizard (one step rendered at a time, Back/Next footer) while the Standard editor rendered all three regions (Logic → Targeting → Output & Run) as siblings in one continuous scroll-jump form — so learning one built the wrong mental model for the other. **PO decision (2026-07-17): convert, not visually differentiate.** The Standard editor now renders exactly one step at a time via the shared numbered step-pill strip (`aria-current="step"`), with an adaptive footer (Cancel · Back · Save · Next; Next becomes the primary Save on the last step). The whole-rule synthesis rail (live plain-language intent sentence, advisory analyzer chips, save hint) stays **persistent across every step** — it reads full form state, which exists regardless of which step renders — so the operator never loses the "what this rule will do" summary while stepping. Validation display is **routed per-step**: every error surfaces on Step 1 (the rule name in the always-visible header, plus conditions and actions in the Logic step), so pressing Save from any step routes back to Step 1 with the error visible and focused. There is **no Review step** (unlike Event Sync's step 4): the Standard rule has no Preview/write gate to reach first and the persistent rail already reviews the whole rule continuously, so a Review step would only duplicate the rail. **Save is exposed on every step** rather than last-step-only: the Standard rule has no preview gate and its step-2/3 fields all carry safe defaults, so a valid rule can be saved from any step (this is exactly the Event Sync editor's editing-an-existing-rule footer, applied uniformly). **No rule behavior, validation logic, or save-path change** — only which step a given field is revealed on and where validation errors are displayed; an existing full-config rule round-trips to a byte-identical save payload (proven by test). The old IntersectionObserver scroll-spy is removed; step focus moves to the active step's heading on navigation. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-09x38.10)
- **EPG Manager: legacy "Dummy EPG Sources" section folds away; Dummy EPG Profiles is the supported dummy EPG path (bead 09x38.4, build 0124).** The EPG Manager tab showed two adjacent, near-identically described dummy EPG sections — legacy Dispatcharr `source_type=dummy` "Dummy EPG Sources" and "ECM Dummy EPG Profiles" — with no way for an operator to tell which to use. PO decision (Option B): the **Dummy EPG Profiles** path is the supported one (it is what Event Sync integrates with, where the live guide-data profiles are authored, and the surface with live preview + richer templates); the legacy `source_type=dummy` section is deprecated. The legacy section now renders **only when such sources already exist** on the instance — on an instance with zero (the common case) it disappears entirely, no empty state and no "+ Add Dummy EPG". Where legacy sources do exist (grandfathered on other deployments) they render as before — visible and fully editable (toggle/edit/delete), retitled "Dummy EPG Sources (Legacy)" with a deprecation notice pointing at Dummy EPG Profiles — but the new-creation affordance is gone, so no new legacy dummy sources can be made through ECM. The supported section drops its "ECM" qualifier and is now simply titled **Dummy EPG Profiles** (loading state, empty-state copy, and the per-row type label updated to match). **Zero data deletion, no migration, no backend change:** legacy dummy-source creation was always the generic `POST /api/epg/sources` with `source_type=dummy` (Dispatcharr parity) — that API is untouched and still reachable; this is a UI-level fold only. The live `B1G Advanced EPG` profile and all five real EPG sources are unaffected. Docs updated: `docs/template_engine.md` gains a supported-surface/legacy-deprecation note (nothing removed — it stays authoritative for the shared engine), and `docs/user_guide/epg/index.md` gains a Dummy EPG section clarifying the supported path and the grandfathered legacy note. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-09x38.4)
- **Auto-Sync Settings modal: the 3 collision-prone sections now name their own entity and cross-reference the similarly-named screen they are NOT (bead 09x38.8, build 0123).** UX pass-1 flagged "Channel Profile Assignment", "Stream Profile Assignment", and "Channel Name Filter (Regex)" as apparent duplicates of the per-account "Manage Account Profiles", the top-level "Stream Profiles" catalog, and the per-account "Manage Filters" screen, respectively — same English words, different entities. A follow-up engineering pass verified all three are functionally distinct (two carry live operator config — "LiveTV" channel profile and "Proxy" stream profile on the Infinity account's "USA | Flo Racing" group) and dropping them would have been a regression, not a dedup; the PO rescoped from deletion to disambiguation-in-place. Each section's help text now states which Dispatcharr entity it configures and why the similarly-named screen elsewhere doesn't cover the same ground: Channel Profile Assignment sets Dispatcharr Channel Profiles (client-facing visibility) for channels synced from this group, distinct from "Manage Account Profiles" (M3U stream failover); Stream Profile Assignment is where a channel's stream/transcode profile actually gets assigned, since the top-level "Stream Profiles" screen is a read-only catalog; Channel Name Filter (Regex) filters this group's already-imported streams at sync time, distinct from "Manage Filters" (account-wide, at M3U import time). No field removed, no save/behavior change. Also fixes the disabled Manage Groups gear tooltip, which named only the Auto-Sync prerequisite — it now also names the group's Enabled toggle when that's the one blocking it. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-09x38.8)
- **Administration → "Security" settings page relocated into Backup & Restore, and its lone control now uses explicit Save semantics (bead 09x38.12, build 0122).** UX audit found the page contained exactly one setting — the backup-destination SSRF allowlist (LAN-friendly vs public-only) — despite its broad "Security" name, and persisted instantly on radio click with no Save button, unlike every other Settings page's explicit "Save Settings" — the wrong inconsistency for a security-relevant control. The card (new `OutboundPolicyCard`, replacing `SecuritySettingsSection`) moved into **Settings → Backup & Restore**, just below the backup-schedule banner, since the setting configures a backup *destination* and that page is its natural home; the standalone "Security" page and its Administration nav item are gone. The control now stages the radio choice as local state and only calls the (unchanged) `PATCH /api/settings/security` endpoint when the operator clicks the new "Save Settings" button — matching the pattern every sibling Settings section (`TLSSettingsSection`, `AuthSettingsSection`, …) already uses; navigating away without saving discards the staged choice, same as those siblings. Bookmarked/shared `#settings/security` URLs keep resolving to `#settings/backup-restore` via the existing legacy-alias hash-routing mechanism (the same one used for the Auto-Creation → Channel Pipeline rename) instead of silently falling back to Settings → General. Doc and in-app copy pointing at "Settings > Security" (the first-run backup-destination modal, `api.ts` comments, `docs/user_guide/backup-restore/configure-cloud-destinations.md`) updated to the new location. Backend endpoint untouched — it was already a focused, idempotent single-field write that fits the staged-save pattern without modification. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-09x38.12)
- **Channel Pipeline: Execution History's "Rollback" and "Undo this run" row actions now explain — and cross-reference — what each actually does (bead 09x38.9, build 0120).** DOM-confirmed UX finding: the two icon-only, destructive actions sat side by side with asymmetric explanation — `title="Rollback"` had zero elaboration next to `title="Undo this run — restores pre-run channel state from snapshot"` — so an operator couldn't tell what distinguished them before clicking. Reading the handlers down to the engine (`rollback_execution` vs `restore_snapshot` in `channel_pipeline_engine.py`) confirmed they are genuinely different mechanisms: **Rollback** is the legacy per-run undo — it deletes the channel(s) this run created and reverts the channel(s) it modified using this run's own recorded changes (or a surgical journal-driven un-merge when the journal fully covers the run), and does NOT touch the pre-run snapshot. **Undo this run** is a full whole-run restore from the pre-run `ChannelPipelineSnapshot` — it overwrites every affected channel's current stream assignments with the exact state captured before the run started, an intentional "optimistic overwrite" (ADR-010 §D5) that also discards any edits made after the run (manual edits, later merges, Dispatcharr drift). Both row tooltips now state this and reference each other by name; the "Confirm Rollback" dialog body was rewritten from a bare one-line count ("This will delete N created channels") to name the legacy-undo mechanism and point at "Undo this run" as the full-restore alternative; the "Undo This Run" dialog gained one added sentence contrasting it with Rollback. The Rollback button also gets the shared `.action-btn.danger` red hover treatment (matching the Delete-rule button elsewhere in this file) so its color no longer matches the Undo button's neutral hover, on top of the two Material Icons glyphs (`undo` vs `settings_backup_restore`) already being visually distinct. No behavior, endpoint, or confirm-flow change — copy and one class name only. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-09x38.9)

### Removed
- **Orphaned status-page components, dead `/api/services` API functions, and the non-functional OIDC "link account" button are gone (beads zipet / f9rlc, build 0062).** The coverage audit found `frontend/src/components/status/*` (StatusIndicator, ServiceCard, ServiceControlPanel, MaintenanceBanner, IncidentTimeline, AlertConfigurationPanel) was an orphaned component cluster never mounted anywhere, alongside dead `/api/services` functions in the API layer — all deleted (zipet). The Linked Accounts settings section no longer renders an OIDC link button whose backend was never implemented, so it could only ever fail (f9rlc). No functional change — none of this code was reachable. (beads zipet / f9rlc), along with their now-dead code; cloud storage targets were relocated, not removed (beads vrrxv / 1w428, build 0029).** Two rarely-used tabs were retired to shrink the surface area. **FFMPEG Builder:** the visual ffmpeg-command builder tab and its entire frontend cluster (`components/ffmpegBuilder/`, `types/ffmpegBuilder.ts`, `data/ffmpegExplanations.ts`), the backend `/api/ffmpeg` router and the `ffmpeg_builder/` engine package, and the `ffmpeg_saved_configs` table are gone. **Export:** the Export tab (playlist export profiles, generate/preview/download M3U+XMLTV, scheduled publish, and publish history) and its frontend cluster (`components/export/`, `services/exportApi.ts`, `types/export.ts`), the corresponding `/api/export/profiles*`, `/api/export/publish-configs*`, and `/api/export/publish-history*` endpoints, the `export_manager` / `publish_pipeline` / `m3u_generator` / `xmltv_generator` modules, the `export_publish` scheduled task, the `playlist_profiles` / `publish_configurations` / `publish_history` tables, and the export-only MCP tools (`list_export_profiles`, `create_export_profile`, `delete_export_profile`, `generate_export`, `list_publish_configs`, `publish_export`) are gone. **Cloud storage targets RELOCATED (not removed):** the cloud-target endpoints moved from `/api/export/cloud-targets` to a dedicated `/api/cloud-targets` router; the management UI moved into **Settings → Backup & Restore** (new `CloudTargetsCard`); the `list_cloud_targets` MCP tool was repointed to the new endpoint (tool contract key `cloud_list_targets`). DBAS backup uploads continue to work against the relocated surface. **Intentionally KEPT (load-bearing, not part of the tabs):** the `ffmpeg_profiles` table (still a DBAS config backup/restore section); stream profiles (`/api/stream-profiles` and the M3U/XMLTV lineup IPTV clients consume); the `SyncTarget` cross-instance-sync surface; the Guide tab's client-side print feature (PrintGuideModal); dummy-EPG XMLTV generation; all non-Export-tab "export" endpoints (`/api/backup/export-sections`, `/api/backup/export`, `/api/dummy-epg/profiles/export/yaml`, per-channel CSV export, normalization export, auto-creation YAML export). Schema migration `0029` drops `ffmpeg_saved_configs`, `playlist_profiles`, `publish_configurations`, and `publish_history`. (beads vrrxv / 1w428)

### Fixed
- **Channel Pipeline rule editor: the action card's remove "X" no longer has a card-height clickable area (bead enhancedchannelmanager-gbwfs, GH #726, build 0162).** Reported by @langtd19: in the rule editor's Actions section, the remove button's invisible hit area stretched to the full height of the action card (423px tall on a create-channel card, against an 18px glyph), so clicking anywhere in the blank space below the visible X silently deleted the action with no confirmation. Root cause: the button is a direct child of the action card's flex row, which stretches children to the card height by default. The button is now pinned to the top of the card (`align-self: flex-start`, matching the Conditions section's remove button), so the destructive click only fires on the visible X. The Conditions remove button, condition-group and modal close buttons, and other trailing-X controls in the editor family were audited and already constrained. CSS-only; no behavior change beyond the hit area.
- **Channel Pipeline Execution History no longer shows a broken "Rollback" button on snapshot-backed runs (bead enhancedchannelmanager-h2oxl, build 0159).** The Rollback button POSTs to `/rollback` without `confirm=true`; on runs that captured a pre-run snapshot the backend requires `confirm=true` for that path and returns 409, so the button did nothing while a working "Undo this run" (full snapshot restore) sat right next to it. Rollback is now hidden on snapshot-backed runs — "Undo this run" is the correct full-restore action there — and still renders on legacy no-snapshot runs, where `/rollback` works. The Rollback tooltip and confirm dialog copy were updated to describe the no-snapshot case accurately instead of cross-referencing a snapshot/"Undo this run" affordance that isn't present on those rows. Frontend-only; the backend `/rollback` endpoint is unchanged.
- **Channel Pipeline runs now surface a failed `assign_channel_profile` action as a non-green run outcome instead of reporting the whole run "completed", and Event Sync rules actually apply their channel-profile selection (bead enhancedchannelmanager-y3m6o.1, GH #720 hardening / Part 1 truthful-status, build 0152).** Part B (the M3U Auto-Sync-modal path, bead enhancedchannelmanager-y3m6o.3) remains open, so this build hardens Part 1's truthful-status behavior rather than closing GH #720. Fix-forward on build 0151: a team review found that 0151 made the *action* truthful (it returns `success=False` when it cannot prove exclusive membership) but the *run* still finalized as `completed` with a green success badge/toast, so the operator-visible outcome still hid the failure. This build closes five gaps. (1) **Run status** — the engine now aggregates failed actions at finalization; a run with any failed action finalizes as the new `completed_with_errors` terminal status (distinct from green `completed` — some channels may have succeeded, so it is deliberately not a blanket `failed`), persists an error count + short summary on the execution record, and the top-level API result reports `success: false`. The executions UI renders `completed_with_errors` as an amber/warning badge and toast (not color-only — it carries the "Completed with Errors" label and the failed-action summary), and keeps the run's rollback/undo affordances. The unattended auto-fire path (the post-M3U-refresh scheduled run) likewise no longer notifies green on a failed-action run: it reports the run as *completed with warnings* — a single warning notification that fires external alerts, rather than a green success (the GH #720 hidden-failure class on the 3 AM path) or a misleading hard "failed" — while the persisted pipeline `execution.status` stays the honest `completed_with_errors`. (2) **Dry-run preview** — a dry run whose channel-profile universe is unavailable now returns an explicit blocking outcome instead of a rosy "Would assign N…", so the preview no longer lies about a live run it could not honor. (3) **No-op write** — a total profile-write failure (every enable/disable PATCH raised) is no longer counted as a channel modification (`modified=False`), so it creates no phantom update count or rollback entity; a partial success still counts. (4) **Event Sync** — a configured `assign_channel_profile` action on an Event Sync rule now actually executes: the dedicated attach phase applies exclusive profile membership to the channels the rule touched this run (newly-attached master channels + promoted channels), the exact "new channel joined to all profiles" case GH #720 describes; the 0151 coverage exercised only a synthetic no-streams path and never ran the real `execute_event_sync_rule` production path. (5) **Rollback honesty** — `assign_channel_profile` carries no reversible previous-state, so it is now marked non-rollbackable and is no longer recorded as a rollback entity that claims to reverse a change neither the legacy rollback nor the snapshot restore can undo. **Post-review hardening (same build 0152).** A follow-up team review found the failure aggregation incomplete and three related surfaces still misleading; all are now fixed: **(a) Full failure aggregation** — every executed-action/phase failure now funnels through the one run-level aggregation chokepoint, not just `assign_channel_profile` action failures. Event Sync attach failures (master + promotion), Event Sync dummy-EPG assignment failures, deferred-EPG (Pass 5) retry failures, and the auto-create default-profile assignment failure (previously log-only — now escalated per PO decision) all finalize the run `completed_with_errors` + non-success. **(b) Compound capped + failed runs** are no longer lossy or noisy — a run that is both capped and had failed actions persists BOTH conditions on the execution row (the cap info AND the failed-action summary) and the unattended path emits ONE coherent warning carrying both, instead of a cap warning plus a separate "Completed with Warnings". **(c) Rollback disclosure** — a run that mutated channel-profile membership persists a `non_reversible_profile_changes` warning, and the executions UI's Rollback/Undo confirmations and tooltips now disclose that channel-profile membership will NOT be restored (rollback is kept for the reversible stream/field changes; it no longer over-promises "all affected"). **(d) Task History honesty** — a `success=True` run with `failed_count > 0` (the PO-chosen "Completed with Warnings" envelope) now renders an amber warning indicator in Task History instead of solid green. Regression tests drive the real run-level status via `run_pipeline` and the real Event Sync attach path via `execute_event_sync_rule`, with a per-failure-type regression for each aggregated phase. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-y3m6o.1)
- **Channel Pipeline `assign_channel_profile` now reports a non-success/partial result when it cannot prove exclusive profile membership, instead of falsely reporting success (bead enhancedchannelmanager-y3m6o.1, GH #720 hardening, build 0151).** Follow-up to Part A (build 0150): a team review found the subtractive-membership action reported `success=True` even when it had NOT actually achieved exclusive membership, in two ways. (1) Partial write failure — each per-profile enable/disable call was best-effort but a failed DISABLE was caught, counted only as a non-success, and swallowed, so a channel could be left in a profile it should not be in while execution history/UI showed success. The helper now collects the failed profile ids and the action returns a non-success result surfacing them (enabled/disabled counts plus the failed profile ids — reported as the run's `completed_with_errors` status from build 0152, not an informal "partial result"), so incomplete reconciliation is observable and safe to retry manually by rerunning the pipeline (every profile-write path is idempotent). (2) Universe-unavailable degradation — a failed profile-universe fetch was collapsed to an empty universe, silently degrading to enable-selected-only-and-report-success and recreating GH #720 during a transient read failure. The engine now passes an explicit "unavailable" sentinel (`None`, distinct from `[]` "genuinely empty") when `get_channel_profiles()` raises, and the action FAILS rather than enabling-only when the universe is unavailable. Best-effort continuation is preserved (one failing profile does not abort the rest); the global default-profile assignment path stays a benign no-op on an empty/unavailable universe. Covers standard and event-sync rule paths. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-y3m6o.1)
- **Channel Pipeline `assign_channel_profile` rule actions now honor the channel-profile selection subtractively instead of leaving new channels in ALL profiles (bead enhancedchannelmanager-y3m6o, GH #720 (Part 1 of 2), build 0150).** Reported by @langtd19: standard and event-sync rules with a channel-profile selection added created channels to *every* channel profile rather than only the selected one(s). Root cause: the action applied the selection ENABLE-ONLY, but Dispatcharr auto-joins every newly-created channel to all channel profiles by default, so honoring a selection is subtractive — the selected profiles must be enabled AND the channel disabled in every other known profile; enable-only was a no-op. The action now makes the channel a member of exactly the selected profiles via a shared `_apply_exclusive_profile_membership` helper (the same enable-in/disable-out pattern the global default-profile setting already used), and the engine now fetches the full channel-profile universe whenever any rule (standard or event-sync) uses an `assign_channel_profile` action — previously it fetched only when a global default was configured, so a per-rule selection with no global default got an empty universe and could not disable anything. When the profile universe is unavailable it degrades to enable-selected-only (never worse than before). **Superseded by builds 0151/0152:** the unavailable-universe behavior described here (silent enable-selected-only degradation) was found to be able to recreate GH #720 during a transient read failure — from build 0151 an unavailable universe FAILS the action rather than degrading, and from build 0152 that failure surfaces as the run's `completed_with_errors` status. This is the rule-action path; the M3U Auto-Sync-modal path is Part 2 (still open). (bead enhancedchannelmanager-y3m6o)
- **Portaled dropdowns remain reachable near the bottom of scrollable editors (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ffdx5, GH #717, build 0149).** The normalization-group picker and shared single-select now use the same viewport-aware placement logic: open upward when possible, preserve a usable options area in short viewports, close if their trigger scrolls fully out of view, and recompute through a throttled animation frame when a modal scrolls or the browser resizes. This keeps every group selectable in the Standard Rule Targeting step and fixes the same clipping risk across `CustomSelect` call sites without changing selection behavior.
- **`POST /api/client-errors` no longer 500s on a reported stack trace containing a URL with an out-of-range port (bead k6ud9, build 0142).** Observed live after a frontend deploy: a browser reported a stale-bundle fetch error whose stack trace embedded a URL with a malformed port, and `obfuscate.py`'s `obfuscate_url()` accessed `parsed.port` on the `urlparse()` result unguarded — Python raises `ValueError` for a port outside 0-65535 (or non-numeric), uncaught, so the endpoint itself crashed on exactly the kind of malformed input client-error reports contain, losing the report. `parsed.port` access is now wrapped in try/except, treating an unparseable port the same as "no port" — obfuscation behavior is otherwise unchanged. Audited `obfuscate.py` and its caller (`routers/client_errors.py`) for other unguarded `urlparse` attribute accesses; none found (`.hostname`, `.scheme`, `.path`, `.params`, `.query`, `.fragment` do not raise, and `urlparse()` itself was already wrapped). 12 new tests (`backend/tests/unit/test_obfuscate.py`, `backend/tests/routers/test_client_errors.py::TestHostileUrlPortInStack`) cover out-of-range, negative, non-numeric, and IPv6 hostile ports, plus an endpoint-level regression proving the 500 is gone. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-k6ud9)
- **Auto-created channels' EPG matcher is now region-aware — West channels no longer auto-link to their East counterpart's guide row (bead vznut.4, build 0134).** vznut.2 (build 0090) made the *manual* matcher (`match_channels_epg`) region-aware but explicitly scoped out auto-creation's separate, simpler matcher (`channel_pipeline_executor._match_epg_data`), which kept its own hardcoded timezone list and no region scoring — so a channel auto-created by a Channel Pipeline rule could still West-link to East. `_match_epg_data` now reuses `epg_matching.detect_region()` (one shared source of regional truth with vznut.2, no duplicated detection logic) via a new region-consistency tie-break applied within its existing exact/prefix match tiers, AHEAD of the pre-existing name-length-similarity tie-break — length is a coincidental signal, region is deliberate. Inert for channels with no detected region, so non-regional auto-creation matching is unchanged. 19 new tests (`backend/tests/unit/test_channel_pipeline_executor_epg_region.py`) split between characterization guards pinning pre-fix behavior and new regional coverage. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-vznut.4)
- **EPG audit and match endpoints no longer silently truncate at 10,000 channels (bead vznut.6, build 0134).** `GET /api/epg/audit-duplicates` and `POST /api/epg/match` fetched channels with a single `page_size=10000` call (pre-existing, flagged as code-review nit #4 on vznut.1) — a fleet larger than 10,000 channels would silently lose channels past the cutoff, with `total_channels` then misreporting the true fleet size. Both handlers now share a `_fetch_all_channels` helper (`backend/routers/epg.py`) that pages the full fleet (page_size=1000, same paged-scan shape as the stale-ids endpoint and `stream_stats.get_streams`), with a ~1M-channel hard-stop safety cap (logged, not silent) rather than an unbounded loop. No extra round-trip for the common small-fleet case — a single-page response still resolves in one call. 6 new tests covering multi-page, single-page, and empty-fleet fetches; live-verified on the running instance (identical 50-channel, no-shared-links audit result before/after). (bead enhancedchannelmanager-vznut.6)
- **Event Sync: recurring dateless events no longer require a daily re-accept in the review queue — decisions carry forward across days (bead t6bin, build 0132).** Under `assume_current_date`, the review fingerprint's event key embedded the parser-synthesized date, so a recurring dateless slot ("PPV 01: FURY vs HALL 6PM" listed every day) minted a NEW fingerprint at every midnight: operator accepts/rejects never re-applied, and the stale-dateless demote rail (jqwfq) re-demoted the same slot to the review queue every morning. The key now drops the fabricated date ONLY when the parse came from one of the three synthesized-date dateless patterns, keying on `<title>|dateless|<local clock HH:MM><standard tz offset>` instead — the standard (non-DST) offset so keys survive DST transitions, and distinct clock times or titles stay distinct slots. Accepts keep auto-attaching (outranking the demote rail) and rejects keep suppressing on every following day; the queue no longer refills with answered questions. Dated events are byte-for-byte unaffected — stored rows need no migration. One-time cost: review decisions made on DATELESS slots before this build re-ask once after upgrading (whether a stored key's date was synthesized is not recoverable); dated decisions do not re-ask. 15 new tests including DB-backed accept/reject carry-forward across a mocked day boundary. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-t6bin)
- **Channel logos now render when Dispatcharr is reached via docker-internal hostnames — ECM proxies logo images and serves client-resolvable URLs (bead hhmat, build 0131, GH-662).** Reported by @FractalBoy: when ECM connects to Dispatcharr via a docker-network hostname (localhost behind gluetun, or a container name), channel icons never rendered — Dispatcharr builds `cache_url` from the Host header it saw on ECM's *server-side* request, so the value shipped to the browser was often a hostname/LAN IP the operator's browser cannot resolve. New same-origin proxy endpoint `GET /api/channels/logos/{logo_id}/image` fetches the bytes server-side through ECM's authenticated Dispatcharr client and serves them from ECM's own origin (intentionally left out of the auth-exempt path list — the browser's `<img>` tag already carries the httpOnly session cookie, so no frontend change was needed for auth). Every logo response path (`GET /logos`, `GET /logos/{id}`, `POST /logos` create incl. the find-existing-by-url fallback, `POST /logos/upload`, `PATCH /logos/{id}`) now rewrites `cache_url` to point at the proxy, preserving Dispatcharr's `?v=<hash>` cache-buster so browser caching stays correct across logo replacements; a falsy `cache_url` is left alone (frontend falls back to `logo.url`) and `url` itself is never touched. The proxy forwards `If-None-Match` upstream (304 short-circuits), passes through upstream `Cache-Control`/`ETag` when present (falling back to a day-long default), and maps upstream 404 to 404 and other upstream failures to 502. 15 new tests in `backend/tests/routers/test_logos.py`; live-verified (401 unauthenticated, successful authenticated proxy fetch, rewritten `cache_url` in real API responses). (bead enhancedchannelmanager-hhmat)
- **Merge Streams "Exclude target groups" and the two normalization-group pickers now use a searchable multi-select dropdown instead of an unbounded checkbox list (bead zi85o, build 0131, GH-677).** Reported by @langtd19: the Merge Streams action's "Exclude target groups" field rendered every channel group inline with no scroll cap or search — unusable on a real instance with ~2900 groups. New shared `GroupMultiSelectDropdown` component (collapsed "N selected" button, search box, Select All/Clear All, checkbox list, outside-click and Escape close) replaces the inline list at all three sites that had it: `ActionEditor`'s Merge Streams "Exclude target groups", `RuleBuilder`'s Normalization Groups picker, and `BulkRuleSettingsModal`'s Apply-normalization-groups picker. Reuses the existing `.filter-dropdown-*` shared classes from `common.css` (the same idiom already proven in the Streams/Channels pane group filters) rather than introducing new CSS. Because this component lives inside scrollable modal bodies (unlike the toolbar filters it borrows styling from), the menu renders through a portal at a fixed position instead of in normal flow, which surfaced two portal-specific bugs fixed before shipping: the menu was painting behind the modal overlay (z-index raised to the shared `--z-topmost` token) and Escape was closing the dropdown and simultaneously triggering the parent modal's own Escape-to-close/dirty-check handler (fixed with a capture-phase listener). (bead enhancedchannelmanager-zi85o)
- **Channel Pipeline rules with a `{date+3d}`-style date token in a regex condition now save — the write-time validator was rejecting a documented, runtime-supported feature (bead qa43j, build 0128).** A user report: entering `{date+3d}` (or even the bare `{date}`) in a "Matches (Regex)" condition (`stream_name_matches`, `stream_group_matches`, `tvg_id_matches`, `channel_exists_matching`) returned a 422 `REGEX_VALIDATION_ERROR` — but `{date...}`/`{today...}` date-token expansion in these exact condition types is a documented feature (USER_GUIDE.md § "Date Expansion in Regex", contributed by @lpukatch) that `channel_pipeline_evaluator` correctly expands and runs at execution time. The gate was wrong, not the runtime: **two** independent write-time validators — `regex_lint.lint_conditions_json` (the 422 path) and `channel_pipeline_schema.Condition.validate` (a second, 400-returning gate that runs right after it in the same request) — both compiled the RAW value via `safe_regex`/`re`, and `{...}` with non-digit content is invalid quantifier syntax to the third-party `regex` library. Both gates now expand date tokens through a new shared module, `backend/date_placeholders.py` (single source of truth, also consumed by the evaluator), before compiling — validating exactly the string the evaluator will actually run, per condition type, matching the evaluator's own per-type routing. Normalization's `regex` condition type is deliberately excluded (no date-expansion feature at run time there, so a raw `{date+3d}` is genuinely invalid regex on both sides). Malformed tokens (`{date+}`, `{dat}`) and a date range wide enough to blow the 500-char pattern cap are still correctly rejected — both would also silently never match at run time (`safe_regex.search` enforces the identical length cap and returns "no match" on a compile error), so failing lint for them is consistent with runtime, not a new restriction. Frontend audited: the Rule Builder has no client-side regex-syntax check that blocks Save, so no frontend change was needed. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-qa43j)
- **Settings content pane now scrolls to top on sub-page navigation instead of preserving the previous sub-page's scroll position (bead 09x38.11, build 0117).** DOM-confirmed: leaving Notification Settings scrolled to its bottom (~2340px) and clicking Integrations landed at scrollTop 1431 of 2149 — mid-page, since the `.settings-content` pane re-renders in place and the browser preserves scroll position across the swap. New `useScrollTopReset` hook (`frontend/src/hooks/useScrollTopReset.ts`) resets the pane's `scrollTop` to 0 whenever `activePage` changes (skips the initial mount, so deep-linking to a sub-page via hash routing is unaffected). The reset is an instant assignment, not an animated scroll, so it needs no `prefers-reduced-motion` branch — no motion is introduced either way. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-09x38.11)
- **Form modals with no way to back out without saving now have a Cancel button — 8 modals fixed, plus a static-analysis regression guard (bead 09x38.3, build 0116).** UX audit found two confirmed instances (Add/Edit M3U Account, Add Logo) where the modal footer had ONLY the mutating primary button — no Cancel/secondary — violating the documented Cancel+Primary pattern (`docs/css_guidelines.md` → Modal Patterns); the header X-close was the sole escape hatch. Two independent misses pointed at a component-level gap rather than one-off accidents, so a full audit of every `.modal-footer` in the app followed. **Fixed (8):** `M3UAccountModal`, `LogoModal`, `BulkLCNFetchModal`, `DeleteOrphanedGroupsModal`, `NormalizeNamesModal`, `TaskEditorModal`, `M3ULinkedAccountsModal`, `BulkEPGAssignModal` — each gains a `modal-btn modal-btn-secondary` Cancel that closes without calling its save/assign/delete API. **Deliberately NOT touched:** `M3UGroupsModal` (PR #681's window.confirm dirty-guard already covers unsaved-changes on close — a bare Cancel would bypass it) and `EditChannelModal` (its own hasChanges + "Discard Changes" confirm dialog guards every close path the same way) — a plain Cancel next to an existing dirty-guard is a regression, not a fix. **Enforcement:** no shared modal-footer component exists in this codebase (every modal hand-assembles its own footer JSX against `ModalBase.css`'s shared classes), and introducing one now would mean refactoring ~50 call sites for a one-line invariant — disproportionate to the defect. Instead, a new static-analysis test (`frontend/src/uxAudits/formModalCancelButton.audit.test.ts`, modeled on the existing `iconOnlyButtons.a11y.test.ts` pattern) walks every component `.tsx` file and fails if a `.modal-footer` contains a mutating (primary/danger) button with no secondary/Cancel button beside it, with a documented, justification-required allowlist for the two dirty-guarded modals and one genuinely non-mutating info view (Settings' Probe Results panel). Self-verified: reverting one fix reproduces the failure the test is meant to catch. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-09x38.3)
- **Header/toolbar rows now have a single defined overflow behavior — no more squeezed titles or clipped buttons (bead 09x38.2, build 0115).** Header rows across the app were sized with no overflow strategy, so a wide-enough toolbar either squeezed the title column to min-content (M3U Manager's title wrapped to two lines and the subtitle one word per line at 1280px — the operator hit this in a shipped tutorial screenshot) or overflowed an `overflow:hidden` ancestor and clipped a button out of reach (Guide's "Print Guide" was fully invisible/unreachable below ~1400px; Stats' Live Stats "Refresh" was clipped already at 1440px). **One reusable idiom, applied everywhere: header rows wrap** (`flex-wrap: wrap` + `row-gap`) so the action cluster drops to a second row instead of fighting the title — matching the already-well-behaved Channel Pipeline header. This is baked into the shared `.page-header`/`PageHeader`, `.tab-header`, and `.header-actions` classes (every consumer gets it free) and applied to the bespoke Guide, Stats, and Normalization Rules Engine headers. **On top of that, M3U Manager's 7-button toolbar collapses its four setup/admin actions** (Server Groups, Stream Profiles, Manage Links, Sync Groups) **into a new shared `OverflowMenu` kebab**, keeping Refresh All + Add M3U Account as primary buttons; the title now renders un-wrapped at 1280px. Journal and M3U Changes headers adopted the same wrap idiom for consistency. Live-verified at 1280/1366/1440. Policy documented in `docs/css_guidelines.md` § "Header / toolbar overflow policy". (bead enhancedchannelmanager-09x38.2)
- **Manage Groups modal no longer silently discards unsaved toggles on X-close/Escape (bead 09x38.7, build 0114).** Confirmed live: toggling a group's Enabled/Auto-Sync in M3U Manager → Manage Groups tracks dirty state (it enables the "Save & Refresh" button), but the modal has no Cancel button — closing via the X button or Escape discarded every pending toggle instantly with zero confirmation, and stakes are higher since bead igqcy made Save & Refresh a real multi-field write that operators plausibly batch several toggles before saving. Both close paths now route through a shared handler that prompts "Discard unsaved changes?" whenever dirty state is set; declining leaves the modal open with in-progress toggles intact, and a clean close (no changes) remains instant with no prompt. Outside-click close does not exist for this modal (`ModalOverlay` disables backdrop-click-to-close unless a caller opts in, and this modal never does), so there is no third path to cover. Save & Refresh is unchanged — it never routes through the new guard. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-09x38.7)
- **Auto-sync group settings no longer clobber Dispatcharr's native config; group saves are now Save & Refresh; MCP group tools work again on Dispatcharr v0.24+ (bead igqcy, build 0111).** Dispatcharr v0.25.0+ made `update_group_settings` a full-row upsert — every field omitted from a row is silently reset to defaults — and a live QA pass against v0.27.2 (bead 478fe) proved an enabled-only ECM write wiped a natively-configured group's `auto_channel_sync` (true→false) and start channel (1.0→null). Three fixes, one surface: **(1) Read-modify-write everywhere.** The backend group-settings endpoint (the choke point for both modals, Sync Groups, linked-account cascade, MCP, and direct API callers) now completes every incoming row from the group's current stored state via a shared `merge_group_settings_row` helper before forwarding — omitted fields (including `auto_sync_channel_end`, new in v0.25.0 and never previously sent by ECM) are preserved, explicit nulls still clear. The Event Sync guided toggle uses the same helper. The M3U Groups modal sends complete rows (now incl. `auto_sync_channel_end` and full linked-cascade rows), and the per-group Auto-Sync Settings modal merges `custom_properties` over the group's current dict instead of rebuilding it from an ECM-known-keys whitelist — unknown keys Dispatcharr's sync consumes (`channel_numbering_mode`, `channel_numbering_fallback`, `name_match_exclude_regex`, `force_dummy_epg`, …) survive every save verbatim. **(2) Save & Refresh (Dispatcharr parity).** Dispatcharr's own groups modal only offers "Save and Refresh", so ECM saves used to look inert by comparison. The Manage Groups save button is now "Save & Refresh": after a successful settings save it starts an M3U refresh of the account (and any linked accounts it cascaded to), with a toast confirming the refresh started; a save failure never triggers a refresh. The backend endpoint's semantics are unchanged (no implicit refresh — parity with Dispatcharr's API), and the MCP tools gain an optional `trigger_refresh` flag (default false, response notes the change waits for the next refresh). **(3) MCP group name resolution.** Dispatcharr's serializer stopped providing `name` on `channel_groups[]` in v0.24.0, so `update_m3u_group_settings` / `bulk_update_m3u_group_settings` returned "group not found" for every call; names are now resolved by joining `channel_group` ids against the channel-groups list (pre-v0.24 embedded names still short-circuit the extra fetch), and both tools send complete rows. Regression tests recorded from the real v0.27.2 payload shapes pin all of it (backend 11, MCP 8, frontend 7 across both modals). Live-verified end-to-end against Dispatcharr v0.27.2: a native full row (auto-sync on, start 1, end 500, unknown custom_properties keys) survived an enabled-only API save, the guided toggle, and a UI Save & Refresh untouched; the chained refresh ran with zero channels created; systems restored to snapshot. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-igqcy)
- **Entrypoint hardening: the numeric env expansions on the uvicorn exec line are now quoted and digit-validated, failing closed to their defaults (bead 1xoiq, build 0110).** Follow-up from the wadu3 code review and the new style-guide Shell Scripting rule (build 0109): `ECM_PORT`, `ECM_LIMIT_CONCURRENCY`, and `ECM_TIMEOUT_KEEP_ALIVE` were expanded unquoted on `backend/entrypoint.sh`'s `exec gosu appuser uvicorn` line — the same word-splitting/glob-expansion class as the `ECM_UVICORN_LOOP` blocker fixed in wadu3 (a crafted value containing spaces could smuggle extra uvicorn flags into the argv; a `*` could pathname-expand against `/app`). All three expansions are now double-quoted, and each value is whitelist-validated to digits before the exec: an invalid (non-numeric) value logs a warning naming the rejected value and falls back to the default (6100 / 100 / 30) instead of exiting — under `set -e` + `exec`, an exit would crash-loop the container with the same bad env var set on every restart. Valid overrides and the defaults behave exactly as before; empty values still silently take the defaults. The wadu3 behavioral shell-test harness (runs the real launch tail of `entrypoint.sh` in a POSIX shell and asserts the exact argv uvicorn receives) now also covers the three numeric args: valid pass-through, non-numeric fallback + warning, empty values, flag-injection word-split attempts, glob non-expansion, and text-level quoting guards. Container-verified: clean startup, `--port 6100 --loop asyncio --limit-concurrency 100 --timeout-keep-alive 30` on the live argv, `/api/health` 200. Also clears the last three shellcheck SC2086 findings in the script. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-1xoiq)
- **Event Sync: stale dateless stream names no longer auto-attach to today's masters under `assume_current_date` — new snapshot-backed staleness guard routes them to the review queue (bead jqwfq, build 0107).** Field report (follow-up to the yjchp bundle): a provider left YESTERDAY's dateless slot name in the M3U; `assume_current_date` synthesized TODAY's date at that clock time, so identical title + tiny time delta scored 1.0 and attached the stale stream to the wrong master — undecidable from the name alone. The missing fact — *was this exact name already in the playlist before today?* — is now answered from the M3U change monitor's `M3USnapshot` per-group stream-name lists (Dispatcharr's per-stream `updated_at`/`last_seen` are deliberately NOT used: they bump for every stream on every refresh). **Stage 1 (observability):** every preview/debug-bundle stream row carries a tri-state `name_seen_before_today` (true = present in the account's latest pre-local-midnight snapshot, false = captured-uncapped and absent, null = unknown), with `stale_suspect_streams` / `freshness_unknown_streams` summary counts. **Stage 2 (behavior):** a threshold would-attach is DEMOTED to the review queue (`ambiguous_reason: "stale_dateless_stream_name"`, mirroring the yjchp venue-conflict rail) only when the name is POSITIVELY stale AND `assume_current_date` was load-bearing (counterfactual re-parse with the flag off yields no start) — dated names are never touched, and the signal FAILS OPEN (a missing snapshot, uncaptured group, or a group at the 500-name capture cap can only cause missed detections, never false demotes). A prior review-queue ACCEPT outranks the guard (standard accept-upgrade path); a prior REJECT still suppresses. Per-rule knob `demote_stale_dateless` (default **true**, absent-key default-fill so stored configs need nothing; editor: "Send stale dateless names to review instead of attaching" under Date handling) is the escape hatch for genuinely recurring daily events. Live-validated against a real instance: 41/41 provider names correctly flagged as predating today, 5/5 spot-checks byte-present in the qualifying snapshots. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-jqwfq)
- **Event Sync: four fixes from a user debug bundle — venue-conflict matcher rail, cross-rule pre-flight advice, Journal UI category, and bundle auto_run/pre-flight export (bead yjchp, build 0105).** (1) *Matcher venue-conflict rail:* a teamless pair whose titles BOTH carry unmatched identity tokens after the shared cleaning/bridging pipeline ('Lucas Oil Late Models **Adams** County' vs 'Lucas Oil Late Models at **Shelby** County' — different venue, different event) scored token_set 0.9032, cleared the 0.90 no-teams floor, and auto-attached. Such pairs are now DEMOTED from the attach band to the review queue (never auto-attached, never hard-rejected — a true match stays operator-rescuable), with a machine-readable `venue_token_conflict` marker surfaced through the candidate's reject_reason and the stream's `ambiguous_reason`. One-sided leftovers (master series/sponsor dressing, 'at', a year token, 'Co.'-style abbreviations, truncation) still attach; team-verdict AGREE pairs and operator-lowered sub-0.80 thresholds bypass the rail (manual-control contract). Seven pairs appended to the frozen corpus. (2) *Cross-rule pre-flight message:* when a rule's secondary group fails `secondary_auto_sync_off` because that group is the MASTER of another enabled event_sync rule (masters require auto-sync ON), the failure now names the conflicting rule and advises removing the group from this rule's secondaries (or re-mastering the other rule) instead of the stock "disable auto_channel_sync" advice that would have broken the other rule; failure gains a `conflicting_rule` field, check id unchanged. (3) *Journal UI:* the journal has recorded `event_sync` entries (attaches, review accepts/rejects) since Phase 1B, but the frontend was blind to the category — it now has its own filter option, icon, label, and header stat. (4) *Debug bundle:* `event_sync_matching.json` per-rule entries now export `matching_controls.auto_run` and a per-rule `preflight` result, so "why didn't this rule fire on refresh" is diagnosable from a bundle alone. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-yjchp)
- **Dev-tooling: the agent-side version-advance guard no longer false-blocks `gh pr create` when shipping from a git worktree (bead yxtuz, build 0106).** The `.claude/hooks/version-advance-guard.sh` PreToolUse hook always validated version state against `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` — the MAIN checkout — even when the `gh pr create` being validated ran from a git worktree on a different, further-advanced branch (`cd <worktree> && gh pr create ...`). That checked the wrong checkout and could false-block a legitimately-advanced worktree branch, as happened shipping PR #666 (worked around at the time via `gh api`; CI's server-side version-consistency job re-enforces the same rule against the actual PR head, so it was a friction bug, not a safety gap). The hook now resolves the checkout root to validate from the command being inspected — a leading `cd <path> &&` or a `git -C <path>` in the `gh pr create` command — with an `ECM_VERSION_GUARD_ROOT` env override for cases the command text can't express. The plain main-checkout path (no `cd`, no `-C`, no override) is unchanged. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-yxtuz)
- **Merge rules no longer report a same-named in-scope channel as "not found" when the name exists in several groups (bead g0uuf, build 0103).** A user debug bundle surfaced the failure: five channels all literally named "ESPN", one per group — a supported layout, since GH-92 group scoping exists precisely so different rules can keep same-named channels in different groups. But every executor lookup index (by-name, base-name, normalized, core, fold) held exactly ONE channel per key, so a group-scoped `create_channel` lookup could only test one arbitrary survivor (whichever loaded last for by-name, first for the rest). When that survivor sat in another group, the lookup returned "not found" even though an exact-name match existed in the scoped group — `if_exists: merge_only` skipped every stream ("Channel 'ESPN' not found, skipped (merge only)") and `if_exists: merge` created a duplicate channel in the target group. Normalization was a red herring: the miss happened after normalization, in the index. Each lookup stage now keeps its legacy single-slot pick (historical winner semantics are unchanged whenever that pick passes the scope/manual gates) and, only when it fails those gates, scans every channel indexed under the same key — so scoped lookups find the twin IN the scoped group, unscoped lookups behave exactly as before, and the orzck manual-channel gate plus the wy6l5 blocked-manual breadcrumb keep applying to scanned candidates. Covers rename, live-create, and dry-run-create index updates within a run. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-g0uuf)
- **Pagination bounds validation extended to 8 sibling list endpoints — 422 instead of erroring or silently clamping (bead g4z2h, build 0104, systemic sibling of 1a5mf/PR-660).** `1a5mf` (build 0095) fixed only `GET /api/channels`; the same unvalidated `page: int = 1, page_size: int = 100` pattern existed on 8 more list endpoints, where `page < 1` either passed straight to the upstream Dispatcharr client (surfacing as a 500) or produced an invalid SQL OFFSET/LIMIT on DB-backed endpoints. All 8 now use FastAPI `Query(ge=1)` / `Query(ge=1, le=<ceiling>)` validation, mirroring the 1a5mf idiom, with ceilings chosen from each endpoint's real caller evidence (frontend/MCP) so no legitimate request is broken: `GET /api/channels/logos` (le=10000, matches App.tsx's `getAllLogos()`), `GET /api/streams` (le=1000, App.tsx requests up to 500), `GET /api/journal` (le=250, matches the Journal tab UI's largest page-size option — replaces a silent `min(page_size,200)` clamp that would have quietly truncated that option), `GET /api/m3u/changes` and `GET /api/m3u/accounts/{id}/changes` (le=200), `GET /api/notifications` (le=100), `GET /api/epg/data` (le=1000), and `GET /api/stats/watch-history` (le=100, replaces a silent `min(page_size,100)` clamp). `channel_merges.py`, `event_sync_reviews.py`, and `channel_pipeline.py` list endpoints were confirmed already validated (manual 400 checks or existing `Query` bounds) and needed no change. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-g4z2h)
- **Certificate expiry-days / renewal check no longer skewed by host timezone (bead wccvo, build 0102, follow-up to n5zw2).** `TLSSettings.get_expiry_days()` (and `needs_renewal()`, which calls it) compared `cert_expires_at` — stored as naive UTC — against `datetime.now()`, which is naive *local* time. On a host at a nonzero UTC offset this skewed the reported days-until-expiry and could trip (or miss) the renewal threshold by up to the host's offset. Same bug class as bead n5zw2 (`tls/storage.py`, PR #660) but in a different module that fix didn't cover. Mirrors n5zw2's fix exactly: a `_utcnow_naive()` helper (`datetime.now(timezone.utc)` dropped to naive) replaces the raw `datetime.now()` comparison, keeping the module on the naive-UTC convention. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-wccvo)
- **uvicorn now pins the stdlib `asyncio` event loop instead of `uvloop` (bead wadu3, build 0101, follow-up to GH #546).** `uvicorn[standard]` auto-selects `uvloop` when no `--loop` flag is given, at all three launch points (production `entrypoint.sh`, the HTTPS subprocess in `tls/https_server.py`, and the dev `__main__` run in `main.py`). Upstream `uvloop` 0.22.1 (latest as of 2026-07-16 — no fixed release exists) has two open-risk issues: a segfault under FastAPI-in-container (uvloop#706, still open) and responses leaking to the wrong request under load (uvloop#645, a data-exposure bug fixed on `master` but never released). Both are now eliminated by defaulting every launch point to `--loop asyncio`, with a new `ECM_UVICORN_LOOP` env var as a whitelisted (`auto`/`asyncio`/`uvloop`) escape hatch back to `uvloop` if ever needed — invalid values fall back to `asyncio` with a warning. The active loop implementation is logged at startup for auditability from `docker logs`. Load-tested (60-way concurrent bursts, 7,200 measured requests across both loops): `asyncio` measured statistically indistinguishable throughput from `uvloop` (~1.7% delta, within run-to-run noise) with zero errors and zero response-shape mismatches — SQLite and handler logic dominate latency, not the loop. The GH #546 `BaseException` containment middleware was re-verified identical on both loops. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-wadu3)
- **Auto-creation no longer multiplies channels whose names differ only by spacing/case — via a new opt-in per-rule flag (bead 0vao3, build 0100, refs GH #645).** A reporter's `if_exists: merge` rule produced four channels for `eurosport 2` / `Eurosport 2` / `Eurosport2` / `eurosport2`: the merge lookup compared names case-insensitively but never collapsed whitespace, and normalization rules never remove single interior spaces. New rule option **"Ignore spacing & case differences when matching"** (`fold_match_key`, default OFF — existing installs unchanged): when ON, the create-channel merge lookup falls back to a canonical comparison key (casefold + strip ALL whitespace) so spelling variants merge into one channel. Comparison key only — the stored/visible channel name is never rewritten, and exact matches, group scoping, and manual-channel protection all still win over the fold. **Find Duplicates** gains a matching opt-in **"Ignore spacing differences"** toggle using the same shared canonicalization (`backend/match_fold.py`), so existing spelling-variant duplicates group together for one bulk merge. Caveat (why it's opt-in): genuinely distinct channels whose names differ only by spacing/case merge too — including digit runs ("Canal 5 2" matches "Canal 52"); the rule editor and docs spell this out, and a pinning test documents the digit collision as the fold's contract. Round-trips through rules YAML export/import and DBAS backup; Alembic 0034. Also bundles regression tests pinning that `channel_number: auto` intentionally skips the rule-level renumber pass (bead bn0wa). GH #645 stays open pending reporter confirmation. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-0vao3)
- **Rule Builder now warns when "Channel Sort" won't actually renumber channels (bead bn0wa, build 0099, refs GH #644).** A reporter set `sort_field` expecting alphabetical channel numbers and got silent no-op renumbering instead — `sort_field` only orders stream *processing*, and the rule-level renumber it can trigger is skipped entirely when the rule's `create_channel` action leaves Channel Number at the `auto` sentinel (the default). The Channel Sort field hint was rewritten to state this precisely, and a conditional warning now appears in the Rule Builder whenever a sort is configured alongside an auto-numbered Create Channel action, pointing the operator at a `sort_group` action (always renumbers) or a fixed number range as the actual fix. New user-guide page `docs/user_guide/channel-pipeline/sort-vs-numbering.md` walks through `sort_field` vs. `sort_group`, the `auto`-numbering gotcha, and a worked PL(1)/US(400) example matching the reporter's exact scenario. GH #644 stays open pending reporter confirmation. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-bn0wa)
- **Event Sync: apostrophe-family and underscore in event names no longer strand matches in the review queue (bead 79k6b, build 0098).** Two teamless events (e.g. a Flo Racing "HLR Joker\`s Jackpot" using a backtick as its apostrophe, and "Weekly Racing at Utica_Rome" using an underscore) matched their stream counterparts at only 0.84 / 0.70 and landed in the ambiguous band instead of auto-attaching. The LOCALS name cleaner now fuses the whole apostrophe family (`'` `’` `ʼ` `` ` `` `´`) to nothing and splits underscore to a space, so `Joker\`s` reads as `jokers` and `Utica_Rome` as two tokens — both now reach a full match and attach. The team-token layer fuses the same apostrophe family for consistency. Strict-mode (dedup/exact) name normalization is byte-for-byte unchanged. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-79k6b)
- **Silent ExitCode-0 backend crash contained; [EXIT-DIAG] diagnostics added (bead xzdx9, build 0097, refs GH #546).** A reporter saw the backend die mid-request with no traceback, no faulthandler output, and no uvicorn shutdown sequence — `docker inspect` showed `ExitCode=0`. Verified mechanism on this stack (uvicorn + uvloop + Starlette `BaseHTTPMiddleware`): a `SystemExit`/`KeyboardInterrupt`-class `BaseException` raised while handling a request propagates out of `asyncio.Task.__step` **into the event loop itself**, aborting `uvicorn.serve()` with no shutdown sequence — a bare `SystemExit` then exits the interpreter with status 0 and no traceback. A new `exit_diagnostics.BaseExceptionContainmentMiddleware`, registered as the innermost user middleware (runs inside the same asyncio task as route handlers, before `Task.__step` can re-raise), catches such BaseExceptions, logs a `[EXIT-DIAG]` CRITICAL line with the full traceback, and converts them to an ordinary `RuntimeError` so the request surfaces as a normal logged 500 instead of killing the process. `Exception` subclasses, `asyncio.CancelledError`, and `GeneratorExit` pass through untouched; auth semantics are unaffected (the guard sits inside `auth_middleware`). Verified live: guarded probe survives repeated injected `SystemExit`s (500 each, `[EXIT-DIAG]` logged); burst concurrency testing (60×3 and 50×60 concurrent cookie-authenticated requests) stayed healthy throughout. The original raiser of the `SystemExit` in the reporter's environment remains unidentified — this fix contains the blast radius and gives the next occurrence a full traceback instead of a silent death; GH #546 stays open pending reporter confirmation. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-xzdx9)
- **Guide tab stayed stuck on "Loading guide data…" when opened directly (bead ogm9v, build 0096).** Navigating straight to the Guide tab — without visiting Channel Manager first — could leave it loading forever. The tab's data-load effect depended on the channel/logo lists, which the app streams in progressively (paginated); every update re-ran the whole effect, re-fetching the full EPG grid and resetting the loading state, so in large channel fleets it never settled. Guide-specific data (EPG grid, profiles, groups) now loads once, and channels/logos sync in from the app without re-fetching. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-ogm9v)
- **Three bugs surfaced by the 2026-07 test audit (beads 9rjgr / n5zw2 / 1a5mf, build 0095).** (1) Creating and deleting the same not-yet-committed (temp) channel within one batch now cancels out **regardless of order** — previously a delete-before-create left a dangling delete referencing a channel that was never created, which errored at execution (9rjgr). (2) Certificate expiry/validity checks (`is_expired` / `is_not_yet_valid` / `days_until_expiry`) now compare consistently in UTC instead of mixing naive-local and naive-UTC time, fixing an up-to-your-UTC-offset skew in expiry math (n5zw2). (3) `GET /api/channels` with `page`/`page_size` out of range now returns a 422 validation error instead of a 500 (1a5mf). (beads enhancedchannelmanager-9rjgr / n5zw2 / 1a5mf)

### Changed
- **M3U Manager's per-account "Manage Profiles" disambiguated from Channel Manager's "Manage Profiles" — both now use the vocabulary Settings already established (bead 09x38.6, build 0118).** UX pass 1 found the two features shared a label despite doing unrelated things: `M3UProfileModal` manages per-M3U-account connection-tier profiles (`max_streams`, optional stream rename pattern — type `M3UAccountProfile`), while `ChannelProfilesListModal` manages channel-visibility profiles. Settings (Channel Defaults page, Backup & Restore export list, MCP tool inventory) already uses "Stream Profiles" and "Channel Profiles" as two distinct, established terms — but "Stream Profiles" was already taken by a third, unrelated concept living in the same M3U Manager tab (the ffmpeg/transcode command profiles behind `StreamProfilesListModal`'s kebab-menu entry), so reusing it for the per-account modal would have created a fresh collision instead of fixing one. Chose **"Account Profiles"** for the per-account feature instead — it matches the underlying `M3UAccountProfile` type name exactly and doesn't collide with either existing term. Label-only change, no behavior change: M3U Manager's per-account row-action button title/aria-label goes from "Manage Profiles" to "Manage Account Profiles" (`M3UManagerTab.tsx`, preserving the row's existing "Manage Groups" / "Manage Filters" verb+noun pattern), its modal header goes from "Manage Profiles - {account}" to "Account Profiles - {account}" (`M3UProfileModal.tsx`), and Channel Manager's overflow-menu entry goes from bare "Manage Profiles" to "Channel Profiles" (`ChannelsPane.tsx`), matching `ChannelProfilesListModal`'s own modal title. No other user-facing "Manage Profiles" occurrences found in the frontend or `docs/user_guide/`. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-09x38.6)
- **Per-tab tutorial template + `docs/user_guide/` authoring-convention update — prerequisite for the per-tab tutorial epic (bead enhancedchannelmanager-gsnw0.1, build 0112).** No functional change — this is documentation only. `docs/user_guide/README.md` gains: (1) a literal, copyable "Common tasks" tutorial skeleton — title, a one-sentence audience blockquote, a 2-sentence-max "what this tab is for," numbered goal→steps→**Result** walkthroughs, and a "Going deeper" cross-link section — distilled from the existing `backup-restore/` ("Start here" task-router) and `integrations/` (goal-first setup + explicit end state) sections; (2) a formalized, repo-wide screenshot spec — 1280×720 viewport (matching the existing pin in `e2e/visual-regression.spec.ts`), dark theme, numbered kebab-case `<n>-<short-name>.png` filenames under `docs/images/user_guide/<section>/`, and mandatory inline placement immediately after the step illustrated, never an end-of-article dump — formalizing the pattern already used by `docs/images/event_sync/` (`docs/images/normalization/`'s two pre-existing images are grandfathered, not recaptured); and (3) a capture-mechanics note (Playwright against the dev container, representative seeded data, captured in the same PR as the tutorial bead that needs it). `docs/user_guide/index.md` gains a "By tab" navigation table — one row per UI tab in nav order, linking to the section that owns (or will own) that tab's tutorials, with the net-new sections (`m3u-manager/`, `guide/`, `m3u-changes/`, `logo-manager/`, `journal/`, `settings/`) marked **Planned**. Blocks the 15 remaining per-tab tutorial child beads (gsnw0.2–.16). (bead enhancedchannelmanager-gsnw0.1)
- **Backend test suite self-explains the cryptography-42 venv requirement instead of failing confusingly (bead enhancedchannelmanager-vol5d, build 0109).** No user-facing behavior change. Two engineers independently lost time to 7-9 failures in `backend/tests/unit/test_tls_storage.py` when running the backend suite under a bare system `python3` — its `cryptography` (41.0.7) predates the `x509.Certificate.not_valid_before_utc`/`not_valid_after_utc` properties (added in cryptography 42) that `tls/storage.py`'s `parse_certificate()` reads, and the code's broad exception handling turned the resulting `AttributeError` into confusing assertion failures (wrong subject, wrong validity) rather than an obvious error naming the cause. Fixed both ends: `docs/testing.md` gains a prominent note at the top of the backend-testing section directing agents to `.venv/bin/python -m pytest`; the 9 affected test methods (exactly the ones whose assertions require a genuinely-parsed certificate — directory perms, raw file round-trips, ACME JSON persistence, and the already-invalid/malformed/mismatched/timezone-skew paths are unaffected and still run under any cryptography version) get a `pytest.mark.skipif` gated on a dependency-free `cryptography.__version__` parse, with a reason string naming the fix. Zero new skips under the project venv (cryptography 42+): all 37 tests in the file still pass. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-vol5d)
