- **MCP rule tools now accept stringified booleans/numbers/lists nested in `conditions`/`actions` (bead enhancedchannelmanager-3aczd, GH #600, build 0056).** GH #600 reported that `create_auto_creation_rule`/`update_auto_creation_rule` couldn't deliver `loose_name_match: true`, `min_score: 0.75`, or `target_channel_in_group: [912, 913]` to the backend — blocking the documented scored-fuzzy merge path from MCP. Live reproduction showed ECM's MCP server and backend handle correctly-typed nested values fine; the failure occurs when the *MCP client* (some LLM-driven clients do this) sends nested values as JSON strings (`"true"`, `"0.75"`, `"[912, 913]"`). Top-level tool params are typed and get lax coercion at the FastMCP/Pydantic boundary, but values nested inside the untyped `list[dict]` params rode through verbatim and failed the backend's strict type validation — or worse, a stringified `"false"` for a condition's `negate` was truthy and silently *inverted* the condition. The MCP tool boundary now coerces the known typed fields nested in `conditions`/`actions` (`create_channel_pipeline_rule`/`update_channel_pipeline_rule` and their deprecated `*_auto_creation_*` aliases) with the same leniency the top-level params already get: `"true"`/`"false"` → boolean, numeric strings → numbers, JSON-encoded lists → lists (including string elements, e.g. `["912", "913"]`). Field-aware — string-typed params like `name_template` are never touched, and unparseable values pass through unchanged so the backend's clear validation errors still surface. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-3aczd, GH #600)
- **Checking an empty group in the Channels pane's group filter dropdown now actually shows it (bead enhancedchannelmanager-5gvnb, build 0049).** The group filter dropdown always listed every channel group, including empty ones (with a `(0)` count badge), but checking an empty group had no visible effect — the group's section (and its drop target for dragging streams over from the Streams pane) only rendered if the separate "Show Empty Groups" toggle, in a different menu and off by default, was also enabled. From the user's perspective this looked identical to the group simply not being selectable. `shouldShowGroup()` now treats an empty group being explicitly checked in the filter dropdown as its own override, independent of the "Show Empty Groups" preference, so selecting it makes it appear immediately as a valid drop target. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-5gvnb)

- **Cross-instance sync no longer refuses an entire plan just because the source has one duplicate name (bead ia4pm, build 0044).** A dry run or apply could report "0 items processed" for the WHOLE sync — not just the affected item — whenever the source had two entities sharing a name in a name-unique category (M3U accounts, channel groups, channel profiles), e.g. two channel groups both named the same thing. Root cause: the shared preflight gate (`backend/dbas/preflight.py`, used by both backup/restore AND cross-instance sync) refuses the whole plan on any such collision — correct for a one-shot restore's atomic snapshot semantics, but wrong for a continuously-converging sync, where the documented behavior is to skip just the ambiguous item and keep going (as it already does for an ambiguous no-channel-number collision). Sync now degrades a source-side name collision to a per-item CONFLICT: it keeps the first occurrence, flags the rest as excluded, and remaps any channel that referenced the excluded entity onto the surviving one's destination id — so everything else still syncs normally. Backup/restore's atomic all-or-nothing refusal is unchanged. Also fixes a related reporting bug where a dry-run's real per-item conflicts were always hidden behind a hardcoded 0 failed-count, now summed correctly and mentioned in the dry-run summary message. (bead ia4pm)
- **Cross-instance sync dry-run/apply Task History summary badges now show real per-category item counts instead of a hardcoded "1 total / 1 ok" (bead zu2de, build 0043).** `DbasSyncTask` reported the whole sync run — dry-run preview or apply — as one hardcoded task-level unit regardless of how many channels, groups, profiles, M3U accounts, or EPG sources it actually touched, so Task History's quick-glance badges always showed "1 total / 1 ok" even when a sync spanned dozens of items across categories, misleading an operator into thinking almost nothing synced. The real per-category create/update/skip/fail counts were already computed correctly for the human-readable notification message, just never surfaced in the `TaskResult` badges. A new `SyncCounts` NamedTuple and `_counts_from_report()` method now sum those real counts, so the badges match what the notification text already said. (bead zu2de)
- **Stream probing (and auto-creation) no longer gets permanently stuck on stale Dispatcharr credentials after a settings-page save (bead snryv, build 0042).** Saving a new Dispatcharr URL/username/password/API key only recreated the low-level HTTP client singleton — the already-running `BandwidthTracker`, `StreamProber` (backing every "Probe Now" action and the scheduled `stream_probe`/`failed_stream_reprobe`/`black_screen_scan` tasks), and the `AutoCreationEngine` each captured their OWN Dispatcharr client at process startup and never picked up the correction, so every probe and auto-creation run kept hitting Dispatcharr with the old credentials — 401ing forever — until an operator separately discovered and clicked **Settings → Restart Services**, or restarted the container. Saving connection-relevant settings now automatically rebuilds and reconnects all of these in the background (fire-and-forget, so the save itself still returns immediately even if Dispatcharr is briefly slow/unreachable). Also fixes several issues the rebuild introduced: an in-progress probe being silently hard-cancelled by an unrelated settings save now logs a warning; a failed rebuild is now logged at WARNING instead of INFO; concurrent rebuild calls (a manual restart racing an automatic one) are now serialized so a live tracker/prober can no longer be orphaned; and the tracker/prober singletons are now cleared to `None` (not left stopped-but-reusable) when Dispatcharr is unconfigured. (bead snryv)
- **Bulk EPG assignment now matches numbered network variants correctly instead of losing to the base network's HD entry (bead gl3sd, build 0038).** A channel like `ESPN 2` (space-separated) was matching `ESPN HD` instead of the correct `ESPN2` entry. Root cause: the token-overlap scoring signal (bd-a6445) tokenized "ESPN 2" and "ESPN2" inconsistently — the space-separated channel name split into `["espn", "2"]` with the lone digit dropped by a length filter, while the guide's fused "ESPN2" stayed one token — so the two forms shared zero tokens even though they already normalize to the identical match key. The correct exact match was penalized for an "unmatched" token while the merely-prefix-matching `ESPN HD` collected a full token-overlap bonus plus the HD-variant bonus and won. A standalone digit token now fuses onto its immediately preceding token before scoring (mirroring how the match-key normalization already treats them), with guards so ATSC subchannel markers (`WTVT-DT2`, `KOCE-DT2`) and quality-suffix-adjacent digits aren't affected — the a6445/m4hp1 subchannel tie-break behavior is unchanged. Likely fixes the same class of mismatch for other fused-vs-spaced numbered variants (`FS1`/`FS 1`, etc.). EPG-match-only — no effect on auto-creation. (bead gl3sd)
- **Bulk EPG assignment now matches channels named after a network's short acronym, and match results no longer go stale after an EPG source's data changes (beads 6rz70 / 41pcv, build 0037).** Two fixes. **(1) Acronym matching:** many guide sources encode a network's canonical short id as a parenthetical inside the `tvg_id` (e.g. `FamilyEntertainmentTelevision(FETV).us`, `Home&GardenTelevision(HGTV).us`, `OprahWinfreyNetwork(OWN).us`) or inside the display name. The matcher previously flattened the whole `tvg_id` into one string, burying the acronym mid-word where prefix matching could never find it — so a channel literally named after the acronym (`FETV`, `HGTV`, `OWN`) could get zero candidates, or only a wrong low-confidence one. The matcher now extracts that parenthetical acronym from both `tvg_id` and the display name and matches it exactly against the channel's normalized name (new `match_type="acronym"`, weighted equal to an exact name match); the existing country/league/token-overlap scoring still ranks among acronym matches the same way it does for any other match type. **(2) Stale match cache:** the `/api/epg/match` response cache (5-minute TTL) was keyed only on the requested channel/source ids and source priority order — never on the EPG source's actual data — so editing an EPG source in place (e.g. swapping its feed URL) or letting a scheduled refresh pull new data left a same-selection match request serving the pre-change response, TVG-IDs and all, until the cache aged out. Updating, deleting, or completing a refresh of an EPG source now busts the cached match responses immediately. EPG-match-only — no effect on auto-creation. (beads 6rz70, 41pcv)
- **Bulk EPG assignment now honors the selected EPG source and ranks the clean primary feed over noisy subchannels (bead m4hp1, build 0036).** Two fixes. **(1) Selected EPG source is now enforced:** the modal's chosen source(s) were forwarded to Dispatcharr as a query filter but never enforced in ECM, so if Dispatcharr ignored it you got candidates from every source. ECM now drops any candidate that isn't from a selected source. **(2) Ranking prefers the clean, primary-feed entry:** for over-the-air call-sign matches that previously tied, the matcher was picking longer/noisier entries — e.g. `70 | ABC: KABC Los Angeles` matched `TW BAK KABC B/O (ABC)`, `75 | MY: KCOP Los Angeles` matched `KCOP-DT4 (HEROICN)`, and `77 | PBS: KOCE Los Angeles` matched `KOCE-DT2 (PBS)` over `KOCE-DT (PBS)`. The misleading name-length term was removed from both the confidence score and the sort tiebreak; the token score is now **precision-aware** (it penalizes extra EPG tokens like `TW BAK`/`Stream`/`HEROICN`/`THENEST`, not just rewards shared ones); and a **main-subchannel tiebreak** prefers the primary feed (`-DT`/no-suffix) over diginets (`-DT2`/`-DT3`/`-DT4`) when candidates are otherwise tied. These cases now resolve to `KABC-DT (ABC)`, `KCOP-DT`, and `KOCE-DT (PBS)`; the prior WTVT/KEYE fixes and the exact/country/league hierarchy still hold. EPG-match-only — no effect on auto-creation. (bead m4hp1)
- **Bulk EPG assignment now ranks matches by shared call-sign + network/affiliate tokens, not coincidental name length (bead a6445, build 0035).** A channel like `64 | FOX: WTVT Tampa` was matching `WTVT-DT2 (MVIES)` ahead of the correct `WTVT-DT (FOX)` — both share the call sign `WTVT`, but the wrong subchannel happened to normalize to the same character length as the channel name, and the matcher's confidence score rewarded that coincidental length (+20) while nothing rewarded the shared `FOX` network token. The confidence scoring now adds a **token-overlap signal** (12 pts per shared meaningful token, capped) and reduces the name-length-similarity weight (20 → 10), so an EPG entry that shares more of the channel's real tokens — call sign *and* network/affiliate/city — outranks one that shares only the call sign. Subchannel markers (`DT`, `DT2`) and bare channel numbers are excluded from the token set so they can't create spurious overlap. The existing match hierarchy (exact / country / league preferences) is unchanged and still dominates. EPG-match-only — no effect on auto-creation. (bead a6445)
- **Bulk EPG assignment now ignores channel-number noise in names and uses ECM's one shared normalization engine (bead xxzxe, build 0034).** Bulk EPG matching was missing obvious matches because its name-cleaning was a separate, weaker copy that only stripped a channel number when it was pipe-delimited (`535 | ESPN`) — so `201 ESPN HD` normalized to `201espn` and never matched the guide's `ESPN`, and `ESPN (201)` became `espn201`. Matching now routes through the shared `NormalizationEngine` (the same path auto-creation and Test Rules use — one engine for everything), and the engine's channel-number stripping was extended to also handle number-then-space prefixes (`201 ESPN` → `ESPN`) and trailing parenthesized numbers (`ESPN (201)` → `ESPN`), unconditionally for all consumers. Names whose number is part of the name (`Fox 5`, `CBS 2`, `WGN 9`) and leading-digit brands (`3ABN`, `9Gem`) are preserved. Timezone stripping (needed so `ESPN East` matches the guide's `ESPN`) is applied **only in matching mode**, so auto-creation still keeps `ESPN East`/`ESPN West` as distinct channels. The EPG match path is also ~5–10× faster (shared engine + per-batch memo vs the old per-entry regex loop). The duplicate client-side matcher (`frontend/src/utils/epgMatching.ts`) was already dead code and is slated for removal separately. (bead xxzxe)
- **Scheduled auto-creation is now OPT-IN (disabled by default) — it no longer starts firing autonomously after an upgrade (bead i2xad, build 0033).** ADR-011 Phase 2 (build `v0.17.6-0002`) turned auto-creation from a button-only (MANUAL) task into a self-firing ~60-second task **seeded enabled**, and shipped a startup migration that flipped every existing instance's persisted `auto_creation` task `manual → interval` while leaving it enabled. The combined effect on upgrade was that auto-creation began running on its own on every instance — operators reported it "kicking off all of a sudden" on production. The decoupling architecture itself is fine and is **preserved** (the 60s interval cadence, the AUTO-FIRE GUARD, the GH #473 created-channel cap, the exo4j circuit breaker, and `run_on_refresh` semantics are all unchanged); the regression was purely the **default**. Now the `auto_creation` task ships **disabled by default** (`default_enabled = False`): a fresh install never auto-fires until an operator turns the task on with the single **Enabled** toggle in Settings → Tasks (the 60-second schedule underneath stays in place, so one toggle is all it takes; the choice persists across restarts). A one-time, idempotent startup migration also **auto-corrects already-affected instances** by disabling the auto_creation task once on upgrade — so instances already flipped-and-enabled stop firing as soon as they take this build, with no manual step. (This corrective is intentionally a `_run_migrations` data migration rather than an Alembic revision — the smart-bootstrap fast-path would silently skip a data-only Alembic migration on exactly the upgraded installs that need it — and it is gated by a persisted one-time marker so it never re-disables an operator who later opts back in.) **Operator note:** the corrective disable is unconditional — an operator who had *deliberately* enabled scheduled auto-creation will find it disabled after this upgrade (we cannot distinguish a deliberate enable from the earlier migration-driven enable) and should simply re-enable it once under Settings → Tasks; the choice then persists. Supersedes the ADR-011 §D2 "enabled by default" rollout decision (ADR-011 amended). (bead i2xad)
- **The "Where can ECM send backups?" prompt no longer appears on login — it now fires only when a user actively configures backups (bead s5a3o, build 0032).** The first-run backup-destination choice previously mounted on every login for anyone who hadn't answered it, gated only on a per-browser flag with no relation to whether backups existed — which wrongly implied backups were already running automatically. The unconditional login mount is removed; the prompt now appears only when the user takes a real backup-configuration action: **adding/saving a cloud upload target** or **creating/enabling a backup schedule** (whichever comes first). The existing "answered once → never re-prompt" behavior is preserved (so operators who already responded are unaffected), the safe `lan_friendly` default still applies on dismissal, and the choice remains changeable under Settings → Security. No backend change was needed. (bead s5a3o, successor to nngkg)
- **Gracenote ID retrieval from EPG now reads `<gnid>` (modern XMLTV) with `<lcn>` as a legacy fallback (bead nq9mg, build 0031).** The "Fetch Gracenote IDs" action silently returned nothing because it only looked for an `<lcn>` child element of each XMLTV `<channel>`, and upstream EPG sources have stopped populating `<lcn>` — they now carry the Gracenote station id in a `<gnid>` element. Both the single (`GET /api/epg/lcn`) and batch (`POST /api/epg/lcn/batch`) parse helpers in `backend/routers/epg.py` now prefer `<gnid>` and fall back to `<lcn>` for legacy EPGs (gnid wins regardless of document order; whitespace-only values are ignored). The API response key is unchanged (still `lcn`), so the channel `tvc_guide_stationid` write path is unaffected. (bead nq9mg)
- **Stats tab "Bitrate by Provider" undercounted concurrent sessions and hid HDHomeRun/local-tuner traffic (bead oj02b, build 0030).** Two distinct defects in the per-provider bitrate chart. **(1) Concurrent-session SUM bug:** when several genuinely-distinct sessions watched the *same channel* in the same poll tick, the aggregation collapsed them with `MAX(bytes_delta)` and reported only the single largest stream — e.g. five concurrent 7/4/7/4/20 Mbps sessions on one provider showed **20 Mbps instead of 42**. The `_distinct_provider_poll_subquery` is now a two-stage aggregation: stage 1 dedupes multi-client-per-session overcount (`MAX` grouped by session), stage 2 `SUM`s `bytes_delta` across the distinct sessions of a channel-tick while keeping **one** poll interval per tick (summing the interval too would have reported the per-session *average*, ~8 Mbps, not the aggregate). The provider watch-time and heatmap endpoints are unaffected. **(2) HDHomeRun/local-tuner traffic mislabeled:** streams with no M3U account (HDHomeRun and other local tuners) resolved to `provider_id = NULL` and were folded into the single "Unknown" bucket, so a concurrent HDHomeRun session never appeared as its own line. Such telemetry rows are now tagged at ingest with a synthetic local-tuner identity (negative sentinel `provider_id`, no schema migration needed) and surface as their own **"HD HomeRun"** series; genuinely-unresolved providers still group under "Unknown". Tagging is forward-only — pre-existing NULL rows stay "Unknown" (they can't be reliably reclassified after the fact). (bead oj02b)
- **Test-validity audit remediation — frontend, wave 3 (epic ulp7q, build 0022).** Internal-only. Implemented the 8 `expect(true).toBe(true)` placeholders in `useAuth.test.tsx` as real auth-state tests (redirect / spinner / authed / login), deleted 3 no-op "tested via integration" markers, and turned two vacuous `toBeDefined` hook tests into real ones (auto-refresh timer re-fire; `isOperationPending` true→false flip). Fixed 3 hard fakes in the ffmpegBuilder suites (asserting behavior that either didn't exist or couldn't fail) and tightened ScheduleEditor role-count + ffmpegBuilder substring assertions to exact values. vitest 1662 passed, 0 `act()` warnings. (epic ulp7q, beads .5/.6)
- **Test-validity audit remediation — backend, wave 2 (epic ulp7q, build 0021).** Internal-only. The ffmpeg validate/generate-command/capabilities tests mocked the very functions under test (so the real validation/command-generation path had zero coverage) — rewritten to exercise ECM's real logic; two now-dead `isinstance(result, dict)` test-accommodation branches removed from `backend/routers/ffmpeg.py` (behavior-preserving). Swept ~70 router passthrough tests that asserted only `status_code == 200` to also assert the forwarded body + call args (a handler mutated to `return {}` no longer passes). Fixed integration `status_code in (200,…,500)` tuples that swallowed error paths, two literally-cannot-fail tests (`assert True` / commented-out body), and backend-unit hygiene (real-session row assertions, deterministic frozen-time schedule checks, croniter fallback coverage). Backend suite 6201 passed. (epic ulp7q, beads .2/.3/.4/.7)
- **Test-validity audit remediation — MCP, wave 1 (epic ulp7q, build 0020).** Internal-only, no user-facing change. A suite-wide audit for "fake tests" (pass but don't verify behavior) found two MCP tests that had gone *signal-blind* — they no longer exercised the code path they claimed to. Fixed: `clear_auto_created` tests now supply a real confirm-token and drive the actual clear path (asserting exact "Cleared N ... in M group(s)" wording) instead of silently hitting the preview path; added a test pinning that `match_channels_epg` omits the deprecated `source_order` from the request body. Plus a sweep of ~13 weak MCP assertions (bare-digit / "Error"-only / source-text greps) tightened to assert the tool's built request token or labelled output. (epic ulp7q, beads .1/.8)
- **Frontend test suite is now React `act()`-warning clean (bead bmtoc, build 0019).** Internal-only, no user-facing change: cleared ~88 `act()` async-state warnings across 8 test files (`BackupRestoreSection`, `PreviewStreamModal`, `M3UChangesTab`, `BackupRestoreModal`, `DbasRestoreModal`, `BandwidthPanel`, `EnhancedStatsPanel`, `WatchHistoryPanel`) by awaiting the trailing mount-effect state updates with `waitFor` (the same pattern as the earlier `PopularityPanel` fix). No production code touched, no assertions weakened, all 1665 tests still pass — the warnings no longer bury real signal in CI output. (bead bmtoc)
- **Frontend dev-quality cleanup (beads 2gg44/v7oql/qpw9a, build 0018).** Internal-only, no user-facing behavior change: (1) `.dist_root` (a local frontend build-output dir) is now git-ignored and excluded from eslint, so a stray local build can no longer flood root-level `npm run lint` with errors from minified bundles; (2) removed the `act()` async-state warnings from `PopularityPanel.test.tsx` by awaiting the settling state updates (all tests still pass); (3) memoized the `epgSourcePriority` map in `EditChannelModal` for consistency with `BulkEPGAssignModal`. (beads 2gg44 / v7oql / qpw9a)

### Security
- **Two latent auth hardening fixes found while investigating GH #546's silent backend crash (bead enhancedchannelmanager-0gt2i, build 0052).** Found alongside the exit-path diagnostics above (see Added); neither is confirmed as GH #546's root cause, but both are real bugs worth closing on their own merits. **(1) `_get_secret_key` no longer silently swallows unexpected errors:** the JWT secret-key loader's bare `except Exception` — which falls back to an ephemeral random secret (invalidating every existing session) on failure — is narrowed to `(ImportError, OSError)`, the failure modes it was actually written to handle; the random-secret fallback now logs loudly (ERROR with traceback, plus a repeated WARN that all sessions are now invalid), and any other exception type propagates as a visible `500` instead of a silent, unlogged auth lockout. **(2) `auth/settings.py` load/save/`get_jwt_secret_key()` are now serialized by a module-level `threading.RLock`:** two threads/coroutines racing to first-generate the JWT secret could each generate and persist a *different* random secret, so requests validated against one thread's secret would 401 against the other's — a divergent-secret race consistent with (though not proven to be) the reported concurrency-shaped crash. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-0gt2i)
- **The MCP static-key service principal can no longer bypass the per-field admin settings gate by restoring a backup (bead enhancedchannelmanager-6n76m, build 0051).** The kgz3k field-level gate on `POST /api/settings` (build 0026, below) deliberately treats the MCP service principal as non-admin, so the MCP key cannot rewrite outbound base URLs (SSRF sinks), integration secrets, or the GH #473 safety caps. But the backup-restore endpoints were gated by the generic admin check (which the MCP principal passes — it carries `is_admin=True`) and write the settings blob **wholesale** with no per-field check — so an MCP-key holder could flip every admin-only field, and restore non-redacted credentials from a legacy ZIP, simply by driving a restore (the MCP server ships a `restore_backup` tool that calls exactly this path). The three ECM-settings-writing restore endpoints (`POST /api/backup/restore`, `/restore-saved`, `/restore-yaml`) now use a new `RequireHumanAdminIfEnabled` dependency that rejects the MCP service principal with `403` while still admitting a human operator admin; the MCP `restore_backup` tool surfaces the rejection as a clean error. Every other admin-gated route the MCP key legitimately drives (channel/stream/pipeline management, backup **creation**, and the guardrailed dry-run-by-default DBAS restore, which applies denylist-filtered settings to the Dispatcharr upstream rather than ECM's own settings file) is unchanged. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-6n76m)
- **`POST /api/settings` outbound URLs/keys are now admin-gated and SSRF-validated on save, closing an authenticated SSRF + credential-exfiltration hole (bead kgz3k, build 0026).** The settings-save endpoint had NO admin dependency, so any authenticated caller — including the static MCP API key — could rewrite outbound base URLs (Dispatcharr / Emby / Plex / Jellyfin) and integration secrets, and the existing `_sanitize_base_url` SSRF guard only ran in the test-connection endpoints, never on save. A malicious base URL (e.g. `http://169.254.169.254/`) was therefore stored verbatim and the runtime media-server pollers happily GET'd it with the stored API key every few seconds — turning the save endpoint into an SSRF primitive and a key-exfiltration channel; the `discord_webhook_url` (POSTed verbatim by the notifier) was a worse POST-SSRF sink. Three fixes: **(1) field-level admin gate** — a non-admin (when auth is enabled), including the MCP service principal, is rejected `403` if the request attempts to *change* any admin-only field (connection, outbound URLs, secrets, notification credentials) while still being allowed to save personal preferences (theme / date format / timezone); **(2) SSRF-on-save** — every changed, non-empty outbound base URL is routed through the canonical mode-aware SSRF chokepoint (`security/ssrf.py`, respecting `ssrf_outbound_mode`: LAN-friendly allows RFC1918, public-only blocks it; loopback / link-local / IMDS / ULA / CGNAT / multicast denied in both modes) plus `_sanitize_base_url` for scheme allowlisting and path stripping, rejecting blocked hosts with `400` (an unresolvable LAN host is still saveable — the runtime client re-validates before connecting — but a host that resolves to or is literally a denied address is refused); **(3) Discord webhook allowlist** — `discord_webhook_url` is validated against the Discord webhook host/path allowlist on save (path preserved, not sanitized), rejecting non-Discord hosts with `400`. Empty values remain allowed (operator disabling an integration). (bead kgz3k)
- **MCP/AI guardrail pass — auto-creation can no longer silently overwrite manually-managed channels, destructive MCP tools require confirmation, bulk ops are capped, and every AI mutation is now audited (epic 7afgg → beads orzck/onazy/p8fx9/vp1rx, build 0016).** Closes a class of "the AI did the wrong thing" data-loss reports. **(1) Manual-channel isolation (orzck):** auto-creation matched a stream to an existing channel purely by name (exact / normalized / core-name / call-sign / number-prefix) across *all* channels with no `auto_created` filter, so an `if_exists=merge` rule could silently adopt a hand-built ("regular") channel as its merge target and overwrite its name/streams/filters. The single resolution chokepoint (`_find_channel_by_name`) now refuses to adopt an `auto_created=False` channel unless the firing rule explicitly sets the new `allow_manual_channel_merge` flag (default off, journaled when used); a missing `auto_created` key is treated as manual/protected (ADR-010 convention). Manual channels are *not* stripped from the lookup maps (that would cause duplicate creation) — they are protected at the decision point, so a collision now creates a new auto channel instead of clobbering yours. **(2) Confirmation on destructive MCP tools (onazy):** `delete_channel` / `delete_m3u_account` / `delete_auto_creation_rule` / manual-channel `update_channel` now preview-then-`confirm`; `bulk_delete_channels` / `clear_auto_created` / `bulk_merge_duplicate_channels` require a server-computed `confirm_token` recomputed from the live target set on the apply call (closing the preview→apply TOCTOU window — a bare boolean is theater for an LLM). `bulk_merge_duplicate_channels` loudly flags a manual target. **(3) Batch caps (p8fx9):** settings-driven soft caps (force the token) and hard caps (refuse with a narrow-your-selection error — never silent truncation) on the bulk operations. **(4) AI-mutation audit (vp1rx):** a `mutation_source` dimension on the journal (`mcp_ai` / `ui` / `auto_creation` / `scheduler`) stamped principal-based at the request layer, with a pre-mutation `before_value` and a shared `batch_id` for forensics and recovery; dry-run/preview writes zero journal rows. Migrations `0025`–`0027`. (epic 7afgg)

### Changed
- **Internal: renamed the Auto-Creation backend module/class layer to Channel Pipeline in preparation for a full feature rename (bead enhancedchannelmanager-mteyy, build 0045).** Phase 2 of a multi-phase rename (epic enhancedchannelmanager-3udrl) — backend Python modules, ORM classes, and internal identifiers renamed from `auto_creation_*`/`AutoCreation*` to `channel_pipeline_*`/`ChannelPipeline*`. Zero behavior change: the API route, MCP tool names, database table names, and the operator-facing UI are all unchanged in this phase — those are separate, later phases. No action needed from operators. (bead enhancedchannelmanager-mteyy)
- **"Assign EPG" now lets you choose which EPG sources to match against *before* it runs, instead of auto-matching against every source on open (bead 0m0nu, build 0028).** Choosing **Assign EPG** from the channel three-dot menu previously opened straight into an analysis that — unless you'd narrowed the source filter — matched the selected channels against *all* active EPG sources; on a large guide (e.g. a full US OTA feed with tens of thousands of entries) that scan is slow enough to look like the match "hung" and returned nothing. The modal now opens to a **configure step**: a checkbox list of your active EPG sources (each with its entry count, plus Select All / Clear) and a **"Match N Channels"** button. Matching runs only when you click it; results then land on the same review step as before, and the review step's source dropdown still drives a re-analyze. (bead 0m0nu)
- **`[EMBY-RESOLVER]` per-call and per-session diagnostics moved from INFO to DEBUG (bead 69avc, build 0028).** The Emby user-resolver logged a `resolver_call` line on every resolution attempt and a `session_inspect` line *per cached Emby session* (N lines per call) at INFO, flooding production logs during normal playback. Both are now DEBUG. The lower-frequency forensic lines kept for "Emby shows User #0" triage — `tier_match` (one line per successful attribution) and the `no_server_ip` misconfig early-return — remain at INFO. (bead 69avc)
- **EPG auto-match and channel-edit EPG search now honor each EPG source's configured priority (epic s5vyz, build 0017).** EPG source priority — set by drag-reordering sources in the EPG Manager — was previously a no-op in matching. (1) **Auto-match (`POST /api/epg/match`):** when two EPG candidates score within a 5-point confidence band, the one from the higher-priority source now wins (a *banded tiebreaker* — it never overrides a clearly-better match from a lower-priority source, and the confidence score itself is never altered). Priority is resolved **server-side** from the EPG source records (single source of truth), and a priority reorder now correctly busts the match cache. (2) **Channel-edit + bulk-assign EPG search:** results are ordered so higher-priority sources surface first (stable within a source — existing relevance order is preserved), applied *before* the result-list cap so a preferred-source match can no longer be truncated out of view. A latent bug where the existing (unused) `source_order` plumbing indexed a `list` as a `dict` is removed in the process. (epic s5vyz)
- **Auto-creation rules now default to matching existing channels within the rule's target group only, and existing rules are migrated onto that safe default (bead orzck, build 0016).** Previously the existing-channel name lookup defaulted to group-agnostic (`match_scope_target_group=False`), which let a same-named channel in an *unrelated* group be adopted as a merge target — the amplifier behind cross-group "bleed." The default is now group-scoped, and migration `0026` flips existing rules (left at `False` by the `0002` backfill) onto the scoped behavior. **Operator-visible:** a rule that genuinely relied on cross-group matching must now set `match_scope_target_group=False` explicitly; the manual-channel gate above is the backstop in either case. (bead orzck)

### Deprecated
- **`source_order` on `POST /api/epg/match` is deprecated and ignored (epic s5vyz, build 0017).** EPG source priority is now resolved server-side, so a client-sent `source_order` is no longer needed; any value supplied is ignored and logs a deprecation warning. The frontend and MCP `match_channels_epg` tool no longer send it. The field is scheduled for removal from the request contract in **v0.19.0**. (epic s5vyz)

### Added
- **Journal now shows *who* made each change — UI, AI (MCP), Auto-creation, or Scheduler — with a per-source filter (beads vp1rx/go1ka/t44t2, build 0016).** The journal/activity view renders a source badge on every entry and a "Source" filter wired to the new `GET /api/journal?mutation_source=` parameter, so an operator can see (and isolate) exactly which mutations an AI client made versus the UI, the auto-creation pipeline, or a scheduled task. Scheduled-task mutations are attributed `scheduler` via the task-engine dispatch chokepoint. (beads vp1rx / go1ka / t44t2)
- **DBAS backup/restore over MCP — trigger backups (incl. encrypted) and restore a saved artifact from an MCP client (bead 0wmeg, build 0015).** The MCP server previously only exposed the legacy backup path. It now has `create_dbas_backup` (triggers the v0.18.0 `dbas_backup` task — redacted by default, or an **encrypted** cred-carrying backup when given a passphrase + `acknowledge_unrecoverable`) and `restore_dbas_backup_saved` (restores a saved DBAS artifact by filename, **dry-run preview by default**, apply on explicit confirm, passphrase for encrypted artifacts). A new admin-gated `POST /api/backup/restore-dbas-saved` backs the restore tool — it resolves the filename under the backups directory with the same inlined path-containment guard the saved-restore path uses, then runs the `dbas_restore` task against the on-disk file (never deleting the operator's saved backup). **The passphrase is never logged, echoed in a tool result, or returned in any response** — enforced and tested on both the backend and MCP layers. (bead 0wmeg)

### Security
- **v0.18.1 cross-instance sync — SSRF-guarded remote-client factory, internal substrate (bead i39wu/1t3al, build 0008).** The factory that builds a Dispatcharr-B client from a `SyncTarget` row (`backend/tasks/dbas_sync_client.py`) routes **every** outbound request through the SSRF chokepoint via a custom httpx transport: `validate_outbound_url` at execute time (resolve-then-connect-by-IP, closing the DNS-rebinding window), the original hostname preserved for TLS SNI + `Host:`, and redirect hops re-validated (https→http downgrade refused). Credentials are Fernet-decrypted only at call time and never logged; a fire-time freshness helper aborts a stale/revoked/rotated credential (mirrors the cloud-target contract); `insecure=true` wires verify-skip + a per-cycle audit helper. The CI chokepoint guard (`test_ssrf_chokepoint_guard.py`) was **extended to scan the sync module** (proven red-before-green) so no raw outbound call can bypass the chokepoint. No new crypto. (bead i39wu/1t3al)

### Fixed
- **DBAS restore/sync: a failure late in an apply could leave EPG sources and stream profiles stranded on the destination instead of rolling them back (bead v1uz9, build 0014).** The compensating-rollback delete map (`restore_orchestrator._delete_dispatch`) registered undo-deletes for M3U accounts, channel groups, channel profiles, channels, streams, and users — but **not** EPG sources or stream profiles. So a restore (or a cross-instance sync) that failed *after* those categories were created could only reach `FAILED_ROLLBACK_INCOMPLETE`, leaving residue on the target. Both now have compensators (a new `DispatcharrClient.delete_stream_profile`, endpoint live-confirmed; `delete_epg_source` already existed), so a late-step failure rolls back cleanly (`PARTIAL_FAILED_ROLLED_BACK`, target left as it started). Surfaced by the cross-instance sync test harness (bead 46pkq). (bead v1uz9)
- **DBAS restore: a channel with no channel number that matched an existing no-number channel was silently skipped, hiding a real second channel (bead i39wu/kcxie, build 0010).** The channels importer keyed on `(name, channel_number)` and treated a `(name, null)` ↔ `(name, null)` match as `ALREADY_EXISTS_IDENTICAL` (silent skip) — but a null number is not a stable identity, so a genuinely different second "ESPN" (no number) would never be created and the operator saw a clean no-op. Now an ambiguous null-number match is surfaced as `CONFLICT` (failed-with-reason in the restore report), never a silent skip; a non-null channel-number equality match is unchanged. Fixed uniformly (it was a latent one-shot-restore bug; under continuous sync it would have been silent recurring divergence). (bead i39wu/kcxie)

### Added
- **v0.18.1 cross-instance sync — operator UI for sync targets (bead i39wu/nnl9s, build 0013).** The first operator-facing surface for cross-instance sync: a **Cross-Instance Sync** card in **Settings → Backup & Restore** to add/edit/delete a Dispatcharr-B sync target, see its last-sync time + tri-state status, flip the **enable/disable kill switch**, and run a sync. "Sync now" runs a **dry-run preview first** (no changes); only after a successful preview does an **Apply** appear, behind an explicit source-wins-overwrite confirmation. The card prominently states the two load-bearing semantics: B is a **managed replica** (edits on B are overwritten by A) and **credentials are not synced** (re-enter them on B). Admin-gated. The sync engine itself remains gated on a reachable second Dispatcharr for live validation. (bead i39wu/nnl9s)
- **v0.18.1 cross-instance sync — staleness observability (bead i39wu/k78ja, build 0012).** The cheapest signal that catches the #1 operator risk (a sync silently failing while B drifts from A, discovered only at failover): a tri-state `ecm_sync_runs_total{result}` metric emitted per run, and an **`ECMSyncStalledTargetDrift`** Prometheus alert (warning, not page) that fires when `dbas_sync` has had no successful run for ~3 sync intervals — riding the per-task last-success gauge that already exists for free. Because only a *full* success advances that gauge, the alert also fires on a sustained *partial* loop (the responder triages via `ecm_sync_runs_total{result}`). Adds a `sync-target-stalled-drift` runbook stub. No dashboard. (bead i39wu/k78ja)
- **v0.18.1 cross-instance sync — scheduled + manual sync task, internal substrate (bead i39wu/5gzg5, build 0011).** Wraps the sync engine in a `DbasSyncTask` (`backend/tasks/dbas_sync.py`) so an operator can run a one-way A→B sync **on a schedule** or **force it on demand** (`POST /api/tasks/dbas_sync/run` with `{sync_target_id, confirm_apply}` — admin/privileged-gated). It re-checks the target's credential freshness at fire time (aborts non-silently on a rotated/revoked/disabled credential, before any remote connection), reads the source live each cycle (converge-over-cycles), defaults to a **dry-run preview** (apply only on `confirm_apply=true`, source-wins), and maps the result to a tri-state success/partial/failed task outcome. v1 serializes runs across targets under one task id (safe; per-target concurrency is a small follow-up). No settings UI yet (`nnl9s`). (bead i39wu/5gzg5)
- **v0.18.1 cross-instance sync — channels + streams replication with a collision-safe floor, internal substrate (bead i39wu/kcxie, build 0010).** Extends the one-way sync engine (`tjaey`) to channels and their embedded streams. To stay safe under *continuous* sync (where a one-shot importer's loose match becomes silent recurring divergence), the sync path floors stream matching at **Tier-3 exact** — Tier-4 fuzzy is opt-in per target via `SyncTarget.fuzzy_stream_matching` (default off) and, when on, a fuzzy hit is reported low-confidence rather than a silent update. Logos are excluded from the routine per-cycle slice (ADR-013 S9 — the logo importer's destructive `clear_existing` + streaming cost don't belong in a continuous loop). Still no operator-reachable trigger (`5gzg5`). (bead i39wu/kcxie)
- **v0.18.1 cross-instance sync — one-way sync engine core (config categories), internal substrate (bead i39wu/tjaey, build 0009).** The engine that replicates LOCAL config to a remote Dispatcharr-B (`backend/tasks/dbas_sync_engine.py`): `run_sync(target, confirm_apply=False)` reuses the **unchanged** DBAS restore orchestrator + importers pointed at the SSRF-guarded remote client (`1t3al`). It gathers the local config categories (M3U accounts, EPG sources, channel groups, channel/stream profiles), **redacts to topology-only** via the shared denylist (no secrets on the wire), stamps the plan `schema_version`, runs a **dry-run preview by default** (source-wins apply only on `confirm_apply=True`), and journals each run. `users` is a **code-enforced never-sync category** (continuous push would overwrite B's privilege flags / lock out its operator); channels/streams/logos are a later slice. Still no operator-reachable trigger — the scheduled-task wrapper is `5gzg5`. (bead i39wu/tjaey)
- **v0.18.1 cross-instance sync — SyncTarget CRUD foundation, internal substrate (bead i39wu/vigbu, build 0007).** First code of the v0.18.1 one-way A→B configuration-sync epic ([ADR-013](docs/adr/ADR-013-cross-instance-live-sync.md)), with **no operator-reachable sync yet**. Adds the admin-gated `/api/sync-targets` CRUD (create/list/update/delete a Dispatcharr-B sync destination — base_url + Fernet-encrypted credentials, credentials masked on every response and never returned in clear), four persisted-state columns on `sync_targets` (`last_full_sync_at`, `last_outcome`, `last_source_fingerprint`, `fuzzy_stream_matching`) via additive Alembic `0024`, and `base_url` scheme validation on write. The authoritative SSRF resolve-by-IP gate and the actual sync engine land in later beads (`1t3al`/`tjaey`); per ADR-013 the sync payload is redact-by-default (topology only) and `users` never sync. (bead i39wu/vigbu)
- **Restore a DBAS backup artifact from the UI — dry-run preview, apply, and encrypted-artifact passphrase (bead 7euap, build 0006).** The async DBAS restore endpoint shipped earlier (`POST /api/backup/restore-dbas` + the `dbas_restore` task) had no UI; restoring an artifact was API-only. **Settings → Backup & Restore** now has a **"Restore DBAS Backup"** card opening a modal that: uploads a `.zip` artifact (the format scheduled backups and the Encrypted Backup card produce), sniffs the encryption magic client-side and **prompts for the passphrase** when the artifact is encrypted (ADR-012 D12 / u81kh — the run button stays disabled until a passphrase is entered), runs a **dry-run preview by default** (counts-only, no changes) with an explicit **Apply** option, shows live 13-stage progress, and renders the terminal `RestoreReport` (per-category created/updated/skipped/failed + the logo-miss banner). A dry-run result offers an **"Apply these changes"** follow-through so the operator previews before the one-way apply. Wrong-passphrase / corrupted-artifact / unsupported-version failures surface the sanitized server message. (bead 7euap)
- **DBAS (Backup & Restore) v0.18.0 foundation — internal substrate, not yet user-exposed (epic bd-0i2vt, ADR-012).** First slice of the DBAS-absorption epic. Lands the pieces the user-facing backup/restore feature will build on, with **no operator-reachable surface yet** (no new endpoints wired into the app): (1) a **streaming backup-artifact builder** (`build_backup_artifact`) producing a ZIP of per-category YAML + binary subtree + a manifest carrying a dedicated integer `schema_version` + a SHA-256 sidecar, built to a temp file (not in RAM) for the multi-GB logo case, with a **non-bypassable deep credential-redaction** stage over every category (bd-0i2vt.7); (2) `CloudStorageTarget` extended with credential-freshness columns (`credential_version`, `token_revoked_at`) + an `insecure` TLS-skip flag, and a reserved `SyncTarget` schema (v0.18.1 sync; lands now so the later cut isn't migration-gated) via additive/reversible Alembic `0023` (bd-0i2vt.4); (3) the shared Phase-2 restore **contracts** (response schema, ID-remap table, durable rollback ledger) in `backend/dbas/restore_contracts.py` (bd-kxuj2); (4) a pinned Dispatcharr **test-environment** harness + production-shaped seed tooling (bd-zqtjj). (epic bd-0i2vt)

### Security
- **Optional whole-artifact passphrase encryption for DBAS backups — opt-in, redact-by-default preserved (bd-u81kh, ADR-012 D12, build 0005).** A new crypto primitive (`backend/dbas/artifact_crypto.py`), intentionally parallel to the static-key Fernet at-rest path: **scrypt** KDF (N=2¹⁵, r=8, p=1; per-artifact random salt) → **chunked streaming ChaCha20-Poly1305**, with an authenticated cleartext header carrying a `format_version` that is **separate from** the backup `schema_version` (so a restore-time version gate can refuse an unsupported envelope before asking for a passphrase). Each chunk's AAD binds the header + chunk-index + an `is_final` flag, so chunk swap, reorder, and stream truncation all fail authentication. **Structural no-oracle:** a wrong passphrase and a corrupted artifact raise the identical exception and release **zero plaintext** on any failure (the partial output is destroyed before the error propagates). Wired at exactly two seams — the backup-builder encrypt stage (`build_backup_artifact`, off the event loop) and a single decrypt-at-ingest gate on restore (the per-category importers need no crypto awareness). The encryption is **opt-in**; the default backup stays redact-by-default. An explicit "include credentials for migration" choice **requires** a passphrase and only re-injects an approved migration-cred allowlist (SMTP/source creds; **never** a user `password_hash`) — redaction always runs (structural redact-then-encrypt; there is no "skip redaction" switch). Passphrase minimum is **12 characters** (API-enforced), and producing an encrypted backup requires acknowledging that a **lost passphrase makes the artifact permanently unrecoverable** (a hard-gate checkbox in the new Settings → Encrypted Backup card, not a tooltip). Encrypted backups are **manual-only** (a passphrase is never persisted to the schedule store). Hardening found en route: a passphrase passed as an ad-hoc task parameter would have leaked into task logs **and** the `journal.db` audit row — added a secret-parameter redaction chokepoint in `task_engine`. (bd-u81kh, ADR-012 D12)
- **SSRF egress chokepoint for DBAS outbound traffic — denylist + LAN/public modes + DNS-rebinding mitigation (bd-0i2vt.5, ADR-012 D4).** A standalone outbound-URL validator (`backend/security/ssrf.py`) with an always-on denylist (loopback, link-local, IMDS 169.254.169.254, CGNAT 100.64/10, 0.0.0.0/8, multicast, IPv6 ULA/link-local/site-local, IPv4-mapped — rejected in **both** modes), a first-run-selectable **LAN-friendly (default) vs public-only** policy, and **DNS-rebinding mitigation** (resolve-once → validate every A/AAAA → connect by the validated IP with the hostname preserved for SNI; redirect re-validation; fail-closed on unresolvable). A CI guard fails the build if any `cloud_storage` adapter makes a raw outbound call that bypasses the validator. The chokepoint is in place now; adapters are wired through it in bd-0i2vt.8. Also hardens the Fernet export-key bootstrap (mode/ownership integrity check, repair-or-fail-closed, never silently regenerating a key that has live ciphertext) (bd-0i2vt.2). The DBAS threat model (`docs/security/threat_model_dbas_import.md`) was re-pointed to ADR-012, lifted out of Draft, and given a passphrase-encryption addendum (bd-ujqze). (epic bd-0i2vt)

### Changed
- **Auto-creation after M3U refresh is now its OWN scheduled task, not a refresh sub-step (ADR-011, bd-ka7j9).** Phase 2 of the GH #473 work, building on the exo4j circuit-breaker (PR #505). Previously scheduled M3U refresh *hard-chained* auto-creation as a synchronous side-effect (`run_auto_creation_after_refresh`), with no back-pressure — the structural coupling behind the crash loop. Now M3U refresh just advances a `last_m3u_refresh_completed_at` watermark in settings (on **every** successful refresh — scheduled or manual single-account — preserving the "runs after every refresh" behavior), and the `AutoCreationTask` runs on its own **~60s interval schedule** and decides *for itself* whether to fire: it runs the post-refresh pipeline only when the task is enabled, the breaker/break-glass are clear (read fresh), at least one `enabled + run_on_refresh` rule exists, and the refresh watermark is newer than the last one it consumed (it consumes the watermark *before* running, so a crash or an overlapping tick can't re-fire against the same refresh). **Operator-visible effects:** (1) auto-creation after a refresh now appears as its own task run (own notification + task-history row) rather than a step inside the refresh; (2) there is up to ~60s latency between a refresh completing and auto-creation firing; (3) a single failed M3U account in a batch no longer suppresses auto-creation for the rest of the batch. The two former entry points are collapsed into one (one breaker gate, one created-channel cap path, one notification style). Manual "Run Now" (`POST /api/auto-creation/run`) is still never gated. A one-time, idempotent startup migration flips existing installs' `auto_creation` task schedule MANUAL → INTERVAL 60s (an operator who deliberately set MANUAL is flipped once; it only actually runs when a `run_on_refresh` rule and a refresh exist). The exo4j crash-sentinel, circuit-breaker flag, reset endpoints, and `ECM_DISABLE_RUN_ON_REFRESH` break-glass are all preserved unchanged. (ADR-011, bd-ka7j9)

### Fixed
- **Auto-creation OOM-killed the container and then crash-looped on every restart (GH #473, bd-sjdsq + bd-exo4j, build 0001).** Two coupled fixes for a persistent outage. **(1) Memory (bd-sjdsq):** the engine accumulated an `execution_log` plus per-stream verbose rule/condition traces in memory for the entire run — O(streams×rules×conditions) — so RSS grew steadily until the kernel OOM-killed the process (reporter recurred even at 32 GiB). `results["execution_log"]` is now a `BoundedExecutionLog` (a `list` subclass, so every append site is governed by construction): non-dry-run runs strip the verbose per-stream trace incrementally and retain at most `MAX_AUTO_CREATION_LOG_ENTRIES` (default 500, settings-overridable) with an aggregate "…N more" summary + a `log_truncated` marker; dry-run keeps the full trace. The run now logs peak RSS and a run-size summary. **(2) Crash loop (bd-exo4j):** a kernel OOM-kill leaves the `AutoCreationExecution` row stuck `status="running"`, and scheduled M3U refresh re-fired `run_on_refresh` auto-creation unconditionally → re-OOM on every restart (doubling RAM didn't help). A startup crash-sentinel now marks orphaned `running` executions `abandoned` (before the scheduler arms) and trips a persisted circuit-breaker that **skips `run_on_refresh` auto-creation until an operator explicitly re-enables it** (`POST /api/auto-creation/reset-circuit-breaker`); a `ECM_DISABLE_RUN_ON_REFRESH=1` env var is a break-glass. Manual "Run Now" is never gated. (GH #473, bd-sjdsq, bd-exo4j, build 0001)

### Added
- **Per-run created-channel cap for auto-creation — a safety valve against runaway rules (GH #473, bd-h2xnl, build 0001).** An over-broad rule (e.g. one matching per-event PPV/event streams) could expand a lineup from ~186 to 2400+ channels in a single run, the upstream amplifier of the OOM. Auto-creation now soft-aborts a run once it would create more than `MAX_AUTO_CREATED_CHANNELS_PER_RUN` channels (default 500, settings-overridable): it stops creating further channels (already-created channels are left consistent), marks the execution `status="capped"`, and surfaces a non-silent "capped at N of M — review the rule or raise the cap" alert. (GH #473, bd-h2xnl, build 0001)

## [0.17.5] — 2026-06-16

### Security
- **Bumped dependencies to clear known CVEs flagged by the release security scans (bd-3hgrf, build 0008).** The main-targeting CI security scans (`pip-audit`, `npm audit`) — which dev-targeting PRs skip — flagged 16 backend CVEs and a frontend high that had gone latent. Backend: `aiohttp` 3.14.0→3.14.1 (8 CVEs, CVE-2026-54273..54280), `python-multipart` 0.0.27→0.0.32 (3 CVEs), `starlette` 1.0.1→1.3.1 (4 CVEs, CVE-2026-48818/48817/54283/54282 — FastAPI 0.136.1's unbounded `starlette>=0.46.0` pin already permits it, so no FastAPI bump was needed), and `cryptography` 46.0.7→49.0.0 (GHSA-537c-gmf6-5ccf). Frontend: `vite` 8.0.10→8.0.16 (GHSA-fx2h-pf6j-xcff `server.fs.deny` bypass) plus transitive patch bumps via non-`--force` `npm audit fix` (no major bumps). `pip-audit` and `npm audit --audit-level=high` now both report zero; full backend (5340 passed) and frontend (1573 passed) suites stayed green against the bumped versions. (bd-3hgrf, build 0008)

### Changed
- **Cut ECM's API call volume against the Dispatcharr backend per refresh cycle (bd-iwfr7, bd-05h8w, bd-1qmn0, build 0006).** Dispatcharr-side profiling (nginx logs + live DB) showed ECM hammering the REST API each cycle. Three connected fixes: (1) M3U change-capture replaced ~1,000 per-group `?channel_group_name=&page_size=1` count probes plus per-group paged pulls with ONE bulk per-account `get_streams` loop grouped client-side; (2) `get_stream_groups_with_counts` now reads `channel_groups[].stream_count` from the single `GET /api/m3u/accounts/` response instead of one probe per group (zero per-group probes; also captures authoritative `enabled`/`is_stale` group state into the M3U snapshot); (3) `BandwidthTracker` no longer re-fetches the ~734 KB `/api/m3u/accounts/` payload on every 10s poll — it caches the accounts snapshot on a 300s TTL (mirroring the channel-map refresh), threaded into the provider resolver, with the on-demand `/api/stats/channels` path keeping its fallback fetch. Stream-pull `page_size` bumped 500→1000 (and auto-creation's per-account sweep 100→1000) to halve round-trips and lower burst RPM. Net: per-cycle `GET /api/channels/streams/` collapses from ~2,283 to a few dozen, and the continuous 734 KB/10s heartbeat (the likely driver of Dispatcharr uWSGI worker memory creep) is eliminated. (bd-iwfr7, bd-05h8w, bd-1qmn0, build 0006)

### Fixed
- **The Channel Manager Streams pane showed empty/disabled groups on first load (bd-rmyn7, build 0007).** A regression from build 0006: switching group counts to read `channel_groups[]` from `/api/m3u/accounts/` (which includes disabled and zero-stream groups) only dropped zero-count groups in the provider-filtered code path. The all-accounts path the Streams pane hits on first load (before any provider filter) therefore surfaced empty/disabled groups; selecting a provider filter "fixed" it. The pre-0006 group universe came from `/api/channels/streams/groups/`, which only lists groups that actually have streams, so empty groups never appeared. Fix: the `count > 0` drop is now unconditional (both the all-accounts and provider-filtered paths), restoring the prior "only populated groups" behavior. Adds a focused regression test. (bd-rmyn7, build 0007)
- **Pressing the spacebar while searching in any dropdown selected the highlighted item instead of typing a space, making it impossible to search for options whose names contain spaces (GH #489, bd-jc4v7, build 0005).** `CustomSelect`'s `handleKeyDown` is bound on the container `<div>` and treated `Enter` and `' '` (Space) identically — `preventDefault()` + select the highlighted option. Because the searchable variant's `<input>` lives inside that container, a space typed into the search box bubbled up to the handler, got its default suppressed, and triggered a selection — so typing e.g. `us | News` auto-selected whatever was highlighted after `us`. This affected **all** searchable `CustomSelect` dropdowns (Scope Group, Normalized Match in Group, M3U-account and channel modals, alert config, etc.). Fix: Space is split out of the `Enter` case and now only opens/selects when focus is on the trigger button (`event.target !== searchInputRef.current`); when the search input is focused it falls through and inserts a literal space. Enter-to-select while searching and arrow/Escape/Home/End navigation are unchanged. Adds the first `CustomSelect` test file covering space-types-into-search (no selection), Enter-still-selects, and Space-opens-from-trigger. (GH #489, bd-jc4v7, build 0005)
- **Large auto-creation runs committed one merge-journal row per merge, churning the SQLite WAL and growing it to very large sizes during a single run (GH #473, PR #474, build 0004).** Each live `merge_stream` audit row (written for the surgical journal-driven un-merge path, bd-0emgo.5) was committed individually, so a run touching thousands of streams produced thousands of tiny transactions. Fix (contributed by @Mirabis): `ActionExecutor` now buffers merge journal rows and flushes them in one transaction via a new `journal.log_entries()` — at a 100-entry threshold and again at pipeline completion — keeping the identical audit payload (`batch_id`, stream-ID-only `before`/`after` values that never embed provider-credentialed URLs, and scored-fuzzy provenance) while cutting transaction frequency. The end-of-pipeline flush is wrapped in `try/finally` so buffered rows describing merges that **actually executed** against Dispatcharr are persisted even when a later pipeline pass (reorder, reconcile-orphans, EPG retry, probe) raises — preserving recoverability of an aborted run. `JournalEntry.timestamp` is now stamped at flush time rather than per-merge. Adds focused coverage for batch journal writes, the threshold flush, and the exception-path flush. Note: this reduces WAL growth but does not by itself resolve #473's large-run memory crash, whose separate root cause (the in-memory `execution_log` + end-of-run `json.dumps`) is tracked independently. (GH #473, PR #474, build 0004)
- **The "Match League Tags" normalization rule's "Only strip when followed by a delimiter" toggle reset to on after every upgrade/restart (GH #484, bd-ov11h, build 0003).** The strong-delimiter requirement on the league-strip rule (`require_delimiter`, bd-0emgo.2) was applied by a startup heal — `set_league_strip_require_delimiter()` — that ran on **every boot** and flipped the flag `False → True` whenever it found it `False`. It was meant as a one-time upgrade fix (so "NFL RedZone" stays a brand instead of collapsing to "RedZone"), but as an every-boot operation it couldn't tell "False because never healed" from "False because the operator deliberately turned it off" — so it re-enabled the option on every restart, clobbering the user's choice (the reporter disabled it, upgraded, and found it back on). Fix: the heal now runs **at most once per install**, gated by a persistent `league_delimiter_heal_applied` marker in settings; once it has run, a value the operator later changes is never touched again. (It must be a settings marker rather than an Alembic data migration: ECM's smart-bootstrap stamps `alembic_version` forward past data-only migrations whenever the live schema already matches the model — bd-5w6jz — so a data migration would be silently skipped on existing installs.) Operators upgrading from a pre-0.17.3 build still get the brand-corruption fix once; everyone already healed (flag currently `True`, including the reporter) sees a no-op heal that just sets the marker, so a subsequent edit to `False` now **sticks** across restarts. Fresh installs are unchanged (they already seed `require_delimiter=True`). Adds gated-heal tests (applies-once-and-sets-marker, skips-when-marker-set, already-true-still-sets-marker). (GH #484, bd-ov11h, build 0003)
- **Stream Probe (and Re-probe Failed Streams, and manual probes) still sent a "probe started" external alert even with only warning/error alerts enabled — the other half of GH #462 (bd-yqnzs, build 0002).** bd-on4sr (v0.17.4-0005) fixed the "starting" external alert for tasks that use the `TaskScheduler` base class's progress notification (e.g. Auto Creation) by gating it on `send_alerts AND alert_on_info`. But the **stream prober has its own separate notification path** (`StreamProber._create_probe_notification`) that hardcoded `send_alerts=True` — a value deliberately set back in **v0.14.0-0008** (2026-02-24, "Re-enable external alerts on probe start notification"), long before the per-task `alert_on_info` gate existed. So an info-level "Stream probe started" message was Telegram/Discord/email-blasted on every run regardless of the task's alert level — the exact symptom the reporter still saw after upgrading, and which they traced to the hardcoded line themselves. Fix: `_create_probe_notification` takes a `send_alerts` flag (threaded through `probe_all_streams` / `probe_streams_by_ids` as `start_send_alerts`, default `True` to preserve any other caller). The two **scheduled** tasks (`stream_probe`, `failed_stream_reprobe`) pass their engine-gated `self._send_alerts` (`send_alerts AND alert_on_info`); the **manual** probe endpoints (`POST /api/stream-stats/probe/all`, `/probe/bulk`) look up the same gate via a new `task_start_alerts_enabled("stream_probe")` helper (info alerts are opt-in, so it defaults to off when the task has no row). Net: the "probe started" message dispatches externally **only** when the operator explicitly enabled info-level alerts — for scheduled *and* manual probes alike. The in-app NotificationCenter toast is unchanged. Adds 3 prober pass-through tests + 4 helper-gate tests + an updated bulk-probe router test. (GH #462, bd-yqnzs, build 0002)

### Added
- **Schedules Direct (SD) EPG support — add an SD account, manage lineups, and pull station logos/program posters from the EPG Manager (bd-ynfal, build 0001).** ECM previously had only a `schedules_direct` stub that targeted Dispatcharr's *old* SD schema (a single `api_key` field), which Dispatcharr migration 0024 removed in favor of `username`/`password` (SHA1-hashed at fetch time). This makes SD a first-class EPG source type alongside XMLTV and dummy EPG. **Credentials:** the EPG source create/update path now accepts `username` + `password` for SD sources; `password` is **write-only** — send it to set or change the credential, never read it back (it is not on the `EPGSource` read type, only accepted on update). **Lineup management:** SD lineups live on the SD account (not Dispatcharr's DB), so ECM proxies Dispatcharr's live SD endpoints — `GET/POST/DELETE /api/epg/sources/{id}/sd-lineups` (list / add / remove a `{lineup}`) and `POST /api/epg/sources/{id}/sd-lineups/search` (`{country, postalcode}` → matching headends). The list response surfaces SD's rate-limit state (`max_lineups`, `changes_remaining`, `changes_reset_at`) so the operator can see how many of SD's ~6-changes/24h budget remain before adding. **Posters:** `GET /api/epg/programs/{id}/poster` streams an SD program poster image (bytes + `Content-Type`) through from Dispatcharr. **Thin-proxy discipline:** Dispatcharr authenticates to SD live on each of these calls and is itself rate-limited by SD; ECM adds **no** retry/polling of its own (in the backend client, the frontend API layer, or MCP) so it never amplifies the SD call count. **SD custom properties** (`logo_style`, `poster_style`, `auto_apply_epg_logos`, `fetch_posters`, and read-only `sd_changes_remaining`/`sd_changes_reset_at`) ride in the source's `custom_properties` bag, mirroring Dispatcharr. **Frontend:** EPG Manager gains an SD settings panel (credentials + logo/poster options) and a lineup manager (search by country/postal code, add/remove, remaining-changes indicator). **MCP:** new `list_sd_lineups` / `search_sd_lineups` / `add_sd_lineup` / `remove_sd_lineup` tools, and `create_epg_source`/`update_epg_source` now accept the SD `username`/`password`/`custom_properties` fields. **Docs:** API reference, Dispatcharr API notes, and the EPG user guide (add-account → add-lineups → logo/poster options → refresh, with SD's 4-lineup cap and 2-hour minimum refresh) all updated. (bd-ynfal, build 0001)

## [0.17.4] — 2026-06-06

### Added
- **Clear Emby Logos — flush Emby's cached channel logos so it re-fetches fresh ones (GH #475, bd-v9tp7, build 0009).** Emby caches channel logos and keeps serving a stale image even after the logo changes upstream in Dispatcharr; the operator had no way to force a refresh. Replicates Channel Identifiarr's "clear logos" feature, **reusing ECM's existing Emby connection** — the saved `emby_base_url` + `emby_api_key` with `X-Emby-Token` auth — so no new credentials and no username/password login (a static Emby API key authenticates `GET /LiveTv/Channels` enumeration and the per-channel `DELETE /Items/{id}/Images/{type}` calls just as well; deleting the cached image makes Emby re-download the logo from its source on next channel access). **Backend:** new `EmbyClient.get_livetv_channels()` (the auth gate) + `delete_item_image(id, type)` with a `Primary`/`LogoLight`/`LogoLightColor` image-type whitelist; new admin-gated `POST /api/emby/clear-logos` returns **202 + {job_id}** and runs a supervised background task (the full-lineup sweep — hundreds of channels × up to 3 types — would otherwise exceed the 30s request-timeout middleware, same 202+poll pattern as bulk-commit, bd-ggxks), with a `GET /api/emby/clear-logos/{job_id}` poll endpoint. The worker clears **all** Live TV channels (accepts an optional `channel_ids` filter for a future per-group UI), is per-channel resilient (one channel lacking a logo type never aborts the run), and validates Emby is configured (400 otherwise). **Progress via the notification system, like stream probe:** the job emits a `task_clear_emby_logos` progress notification (create → rate-limited updates → finalize success/warning) that the NotificationCenter renders live — a progress bar, deleted/skipped/error counts, and the current channel name — driven by a new `clearing` active status. **Frontend:** a "Clear Cached Logos" block in Settings → Integrations → Emby with per-type checkboxes (default all three) and a fire-and-forget button gated on a saved Emby key. **MCP:** new `clear_emby_logos` tool (202+poll, same whitelist). (GH #475, bd-v9tp7, build 0009)
- **Global date-format preference under Settings → Appearance (bd-8j47e, build 0007).** ECM now has a single instance-wide "Date Format" control (Automatic / Month-Day-Year / Day-Month-Year / Year-Month-Day ISO) that drives every date and time shown in the UI. Previously date rendering was inconsistent: `frontend/src/utils/formatting.ts` and ~14 components each formatted dates independently — some hardcoded `en-US` (always m/d/y), others passed no locale and silently deferred to each viewer's browser locale. That split is exactly why one operator saw `06/04` while another saw `04/06` on the same screen. Fix: a single `setDateFormatLocale`/`getDateLocale` resolver in `formatting.ts` maps the preference to a locale (`auto`→browser, `mdy`→en-US, `dmy`→en-GB, `iso`→en-CA), and every date call site — the shared formatters plus all direct `toLocaleString`/`toLocaleDateString`/`Intl.DateTimeFormat` callers — routes through it. The setting is applied on app init and updates live (no reload) when changed. Backend persists `date_format` in `DispatcharrSettings` alongside `theme`. **Scope:** settings are instance-wide, so this is one shared format for all users; the default is `auto`, which preserves the prior per-browser behavior, so no display changes until an operator explicitly pins a format. Time-of-day 12h/24h rendering is unchanged. (bd-8j47e, build 0007)

### Changed
- **`{normalized_name}` now applies the rule's normalization groups in *every* auto-creation action, not just Create Channel (GH #466, bd-6gvt8, build 0006).** The template variable is documented as "Name after normalization rules", but it only behaved that way in Create Channel — which re-normalizes its expanded name in a separate step. Everywhere else (Assign TVG-ID, Set Variable, Assign Logo, …) `{normalized_name}` resolved to the **raw** stream name, because `StreamContext.normalized_name` is never populated during a run and the variable fell back to the stream name. So with a "Strip country prefix" group, `{normalized_name}` produced `CHANNEL` in Create Channel but `US | CHANNEL` in Assign TVG-ID. Fix: `{normalized_name}` is now resolved once at the per-action template-context chokepoint (`_build_template_context`) using the firing rule's `normalization_group_ids`, so it means the same thing in every action. **Scope of change:** only rules that actually have normalization groups are affected — a rule with no groups still yields the raw stream name (unchanged), and Create Channel output is unchanged (its post-expansion normalization is idempotent on the already-normalized value). (GH #466, bd-6gvt8, build 0006)

### Fixed
- **Print Channel Guide rendered nothing but greyed-out phantom rows for groups numbered with float/decimal channel numbers (e.g. OTA 2.1, 5.1) (bd-szsb2, build 0008).** With "Show empty slots" on (the default), `generatePrintHtml` walked the group's range by **integer** steps and looked each slot up in a map keyed by the channel's *exact* number. For a float-numbered group no integer ever matched a float key, so every real channel was skipped (no data) and every integer in the range printed as a greyed `—` placeholder — the operator saw "2–38, all empty, plus channels that don't exist." A secondary bug parsed the per-group From/To bounds with `parseInt`, truncating e.g. `38.5 → 38` and dropping the highest float channel from the range. Fix: a group containing **any** non-integer channel number now skips integer gap-fill entirely and renders only its real channels (the integer gaps between 2.1 and 5.1 are unused major numbers, not "missing channels" — PO decision); pure-integer groups keep the existing empty-slot fill unchanged. The four From/To parse sites switch `parseInt`→`parseFloat` so decimal bounds and the top float channel are respected. Adds 3 float-specific tests (render-not-skipped, no-placeholders-for-float-group, no-`parseInt`-truncation regression). (bd-szsb2, build 0008)
- **Scheduled tasks sent a "starting" external alert (Telegram/Discord/email) on every run even when only warning/error alerts were enabled (GH #462, bd-on4sr, build 0005).** A task's start/progress notification (`task_scheduler._create_progress_notification`) is an "info"-level "<task> starting..." message, but `task_engine._execute_task` wired its external-alert dispatch straight from the master `send_alerts` toggle, never consulting the per-task `alert_on_info` flag. So a task configured with only warning + error alerts (`alert_on_info=False`, the column default) still pushed a "starting" alert to every configured channel on every run — the reporter saw it via Telegram for the auto-creation schedule. The completion alert was already gated per-level (`check_and_run_tasks` honours `alert_on_success`/`_warning`/`_error`/`_info`); only the start notification leaked. Fix: `_execute_task` now passes `send_alerts AND alert_on_info` as the progress-notification alert flag, so the "starting" message dispatches externally only when info alerts are explicitly enabled. The in-app NotificationCenter toast is unchanged — it stays gated by `show_notifications`, independent of the external-alert level. (GH #462, bd-on4sr, build 0005)
- **Deleting a normalization rule group left a dangling reference in auto-creation rules that then refused to save (GH #465, bd-miut3, build 0004).** An auto-creation rule stores the normalization rule groups it applies as `normalization_group_ids` (the "Normalization Groups" selector in the rule editor — the groups that strip quality tags). `DELETE /api/normalization/groups/{id}` removed the group and its rules but never stripped that id from any rule's `normalization_group_ids`, so the rule kept a reference to a group that no longer existed. On the next edit the rule editor reloaded the full id list but couldn't render a checkbox for the missing group, and the write-time validator (`_validate_normalization_group_ids`, bd-i75ax) then rejected every save with `422 — normalization_group_ids do not exist`; the only workaround was "Clear all + re-select". **Impact was save-block only** — at run time `normalize(group_ids=…)` filters by membership, so a missing id was silently ignored and auto-creation still ran correctly. Fix: deleting a normalization group now cascade-strips its id from every auto-creation rule's `normalization_group_ids` in the same transaction (`_strip_normalization_group_ref`), and a startup heal (`_heal_orphaned_normalization_group_refs`, in `_run_migrations`) repairs rules orphaned by deletions that happened before this fix shipped — so already-broken rules become saveable again without operator action. (GH #465, bd-miut3, build 0004)
- **Scheduled tasks showed "Next Run: Never" in Settings → Scheduled Tasks despite having an active schedule (GH #468, bd-a80u2, build 0003).** The "Next Run" the UI renders came from the registry's in-memory `instance._next_run`, which is loaded from the DB only once at startup (`sync_from_database`). Every path that maintains schedules — the schedule create/update/delete endpoints (`_update_task_next_run`) and `task_engine.check_and_run_tasks` after a run — updates the **database** (`scheduled_tasks.next_run_at` + `task_schedules.next_run_at`) but never refreshes the in-memory instance. Tasks whose default schedule is `MANUAL` (Stream Probe, Re-probe Failed Streams, EPG/M3U refresh, etc.) start with `instance._next_run = None`, so adding a schedule through the multi-schedule UI left the in-memory value at `None` and the UI showed "Never" until the next container restart. Compounding this, `_save_task_to_db` wrote the stale in-memory `None` back over the correctly-computed `scheduled_tasks.next_run_at`, so any later `sync_to_database` (e.g. editing alert settings) clobbered the DB value and made "Never" persist across restarts. **Impact was display-only** — the task engine fires off the child `task_schedules.next_run_at` rows, which were always correct, so the tasks did run on schedule. Fix: `GET /api/tasks` and `GET /api/tasks/{id}` now source "Next Run" from the earliest enabled `task_schedules.next_run_at` (the real firing source) instead of the stale in-memory value, falling back to the in-memory value only for tasks with no schedule rows; and `_save_task_to_db` no longer overwrites `next_run_at` for tasks that have `task_schedules` rows (those are DB-managed). (GH #468, bd-a80u2, build 0003)
- **Bulk channel creation re-paginated the entire Dispatcharr logo catalog once *per channel*, making large batches O(channels × catalog-pages) and exhausting the request budget (bd-raehx, build 0002).** Every `createChannel` op carrying a `logoUrl` but no `logoId` called `find_logo_by_url()`, which paginates the whole logo catalog (500/page) on every call with no caching. Two real debug bundles confirm the cost: a 441-op SiriusXM batch issued **2,391** logo GETs (~5-page catalog, finished server-side in ~6 min after a 504), and a 113-op batch against a larger ~25-page catalog issued **859** logo GETs and reached only ~channel 22 before the 30s `504`. This is why batches "created channels but didn't assign streams past ~15", why an op reported *failed* while the write still happened (the handler keeps running after the client's 504), and why adding streams manually afterward worked. Fix: `_run_bulk_commit` now builds a per-run `{url → logo}` index **once**, lazily (only the first time an op actually needs a logo lookup, so `validateOnly` and logo-free batches pay nothing), and every subsequent op reuses it; logos created mid-batch are inserted into the index so a later op sharing the same `logoUrl` reuses them instead of creating a duplicate (also fixes a latent duplicate-logo bug). Catalog fetches drop from ~859/2,391 to ~25/5 — one pagination per batch. The per-channel `find_logo_by_url` first-match semantics and the "logo failure still creates the channel without a logo" fallthrough are preserved. (bd-raehx, build 0002)
- **Bulk-commit partial outcomes are now flagged explicitly so the client can distinguish "some applied, some failed" from a total failure (bd-5xciq, build 0002).** The `BulkCommitResponse` envelope gains a `partial` boolean (`operationsApplied > 0 AND operationsFailed > 0`), present on every return path. This makes a partially-applied batch reconcilable via `tempIdMap` instead of reading as a flat failure that prompts a duplicate-creating retry. The frontend `BulkCommitResponse` type carries the field; existing `useEditMode` rendering already derives partial state from the applied/failed counts, so behavior is unchanged and the flag is exposed for future use. (bd-5xciq, build 0002)
- **`POST /api/channels/bulk-commit` returned 504 mid-flight on large batches while the handler kept running, producing duplicates on retry (bd-ggxks, build 0001).** The endpoint was synchronous and pinned behind the 30s `ECM_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` middleware. A 441-op SiriusXM batch (~6 min of sequential Dispatcharr POSTs) caused the middleware to fire `504 Gateway Timeout` to the operator while the handler kept running in the background; the operator retried, each retry committed another ~30 partial channels, and Dispatcharr accumulated duplicates. Fix: bulk-commit now follows the bd-cns7j / bd-enfsy 202+poll pattern. Non-`validateOnly` `POST /api/channels/bulk-commit` returns `202 + {job_id, status: "running"}` immediately and dispatches a supervised background task; clients poll `GET /api/channels/bulk-commit/{job_id}` until the status is terminal (`completed` with the full `BulkCommitResponse` under `result`, or `failed` with an `error` message). `validateOnly` stays synchronous (200 with the response body inline) because pre-commit validation is fast and the frontend uses it for instant feedback. Frontend `bulkCommit()` and the MCP `bulk_commit_channels` / `build_channel_lineup` tools drive the new POST→poll loop transparently — their public shapes are unchanged so all three `useEditMode` call sites and existing MCP consumers keep working.
